850	  // Length: 2 bytes (little endian)
851	  // Payload: variable length
852	  // Checksum: 2 bytes (CK_A, CK_B)
853	  
854	  // Write sync chars
855	  _serial_conn.write(UBX_SYNC_CHAR_1);
856	  _serial_conn.write(UBX_SYNC_CHAR_2);
857	  
858	  // Write class and ID
859	  _serial_conn.write(msgClass);
860	  _serial_conn.write(msgID);
861	  
862	  // Write length (little endian)
863	  _serial_conn.write(payloadSize & 0xFF);
864	  _serial_conn.write((payloadSize >> 8) & 0xFF);
865	  
866	  // Write payload
867	  if (payload != nullptr && payloadSize > 0) {
868	    _serial_conn.write(payload, payloadSize);
869	  }
870	
871	  // Calculate checksum
872	  uint8_t ck_a = 0, ck_b = 0;
873	
874	  // Add class, ID, and length to checksum
875	  ck_a += msgClass;
876	  ck_b += ck_a;
877	
878	  ck_a += msgID;
879	  ck_b += ck_a;
880	
881	  ck_a += payloadSize & 0xFF;
882	  ck_b += ck_a;
883	
884	  ck_a += (payloadSize >> 8) & 0xFF;
885	  ck_b += ck_a;
886	
887	  // Add payload to checksum
888	  if (payload != nullptr) {
889	    for (size_t i = 0; i < payloadSize; i++) {
890	      ck_a += payload[i];
891	      ck_b += ck_a;
892	    }
893	  }
894	  
895	  // Write checksum
896	  _serial_conn.write(ck_a);
897	  _serial_conn.write(ck_b);
898	  
899	  // Flush the serial buffer
900	  _serial_conn.flush();
901	  
902	  // Wait a bit for the message to be processed
903	  delay(5);
904	  
905	  return true;

908	/**
909	 * Calculate UBX message checksum
910	 * @param data Data to calculate checksum for
911	 * @param len Length of data
912	 * @param cka Pointer to store CK_A
913	 * @param ckb Pointer to store CK_B
914	 */
915	void GpsConnector::calculateChecksum(const uint8_t* data, size_t len, uint8_t* cka, uint8_t* ckb) {
916	  *cka = 0;
917	  *ckb = 0;
918	  
919	  for (size_t i = 0; i < len; i++) {
920	    *cka += data[i];
921	    *ckb += *cka;
922	  }
923	}

925	/**
926	 * Ask the GNSS to enter Backup mode (save state) using UBX-RXM-PMREQ.
927	 * This dramatically reduces TTFF on the next boot.
928	 */
929	bool GpsConnector::requestBackup() {
930	  // UBX-RXM-PMREQ (0x02 0x41) payload is 8 bytes:
931	  // 0-1: version / reserved (set to 0)
932	  // 2-3: duration (little-endian) – 0 means indefinite
933	  // 4-7: flags – bit0 = backup, everything else 0
934	  const uint8_t payload[8] = { 0x00, 0x00, // version / reserved
935	                               0x00, 0x00, // duration LSB/MSB (0 = indefinitely)
936	                               0x01, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00 }; // flags (backup)
937	  return sendUBXMessage(0x02 /*RXM*/, 0x41 /*PMREQ*/, payload, sizeof(payload));
938	}

940	bool GpsConnector::backupGpsMemoryToNVS() {
941	  M5_LOGI("GPS: Starting memory backup to internal storage");
942	  
943	  // Request almanac data using UBX-MGA-GPS-ALM
944	  // This is a simplified implementation - full implementation would need
945	  // to handle multiple UBX-MGA message types and responses
946	  
947	  // For now, we'll use the existing backup functionality which saves
948	  // the GPS state to its internal backup memory
949	  bool success = requestBackup();
950	  
951	  if (success) {
952	    M5_LOGI("GPS: Memory backup completed - GPS internal state saved");
953	  } else {
954	    M5_LOGE("GPS: Memory backup failed");
955	  }
956	  
957	  return success;
958	}
959	
960	bool GpsConnector::restoreGpsMemoryFromNVS() {
961	  M5_LOGI("GPS: Checking for saved memory data in internal storage");
962	  M5_LOGI("GPS: Memory restore relies on U-blox internal backup memory");
963	  return true;
964	}
965	
966	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
967	// NMEA fallback mode — used when GPS TX is connected but GPS RX is not,
968	// so only one-way communication (receive NMEA) is possible.
969	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
970	
971	double GpsConnector::nmeaCoordToDecimal(const char* coord, char direction) {
972	  double val = atof(coord);
973	  int deg = (int)(val / 100.0);
974	  double min = val - deg * 100.0;
975	  double result = deg + min / 60.0;
976	  if (direction == 'S' || direction == 'W') result = -result;
977	  return result;
978	}
979	
980	bool GpsConnector::parseNmeaSentence(const char* sentence) {
981	  // Validate checksum (XOR of bytes between '$' and '*')
982	  const char* star = strchr(sentence, '*');
983	  if (star != nullptr && strlen(star) >= 3) {
984	    uint8_t calc = 0;
985	    for (const char* p = sentence + 1; p < star; p++) calc ^= (uint8_t)*p;
986	    uint8_t recv = (uint8_t)strtol(star + 1, nullptr, 16);
987	    if (calc != recv) return false;
988	  }
989	
990	  bool isRmc = (strncmp(sentence, "$GPRMC", 6) == 0 || strncmp(sentence, "$GNRMC", 6) == 0);
991	  bool isGga = (strncmp(sentence, "$GPGGA", 6) == 0 || strncmp(sentence, "$GNGGA", 6) == 0);
992	  if (!isRmc && !isGga) return false;
993	
994	  // Copy and strip checksum for field splitting
995	  char buf[128];
996	  strncpy(buf, sentence, sizeof(buf) - 1);
997	  buf[sizeof(buf) - 1] = '\0';
998	  char* s = strchr(buf, '*');
999	  if (s) *s = '\0';
1000	
1001	  // Split on commas
1002	  char* fields[15] = {nullptr};
1003	  int nf = 0;
1004	  char* p = buf;
1005	  while (nf < 15) {
1006	    fields[nf++] = p;
1007	    p = strchr(p, ',');
1008	    if (!p) break;
1009	    *p++ = '\0';
1010	  }
1011	
1012	  if (isRmc && nf >= 10) {
1013	    // $G?RMC,HHMMSS.ss,A,DDMM.mmm,N,DDDMM.mmm,E,speed,course,DDMMYY,...
1014	    const char* tstr = fields[1];
1015	    const char* stat = fields[2];
1016	    const char* lat  = fields[3];
1017	    const char* ns   = fields[4];
1018	    const char* lon  = fields[5];
1019	    const char* ew   = fields[6];
1020	    const char* spd  = fields[7];
1021	    const char* crs  = fields[8];
1022	    const char* dstr = fields[9];
1023	
1024	    if (strlen(tstr) >= 6) {
1025	      char tmp[3] = {tstr[0], tstr[1], '\0'}; data.hour   = atoi(tmp);
1026	      tmp[0] = tstr[2]; tmp[1] = tstr[3];     data.minute = atoi(tmp);
1027	      tmp[0] = tstr[4]; tmp[1] = tstr[5];     data.second = atoi(tmp);
1028	      data.time_valid = true;
1029	      time_timer.restart();
1030	    }
1031	    if (strlen(dstr) >= 6) {
1032	      char tmp[3] = {dstr[0], dstr[1], '\0'}; data.day   = atoi(tmp);
1033	      tmp[0] = dstr[2]; tmp[1] = dstr[3];     data.month = atoi(tmp);
1034	      tmp[0] = dstr[4]; tmp[1] = dstr[5];     data.year  = 2000 + atoi(tmp);
1035	      data.date_valid = true;
1036	      date_timer.restart();
1037	    }
1038	    if (stat[0] == 'A' && strlen(lat) > 0 && strlen(lon) > 0) {
1039	      double new_lat = nmeaCoordToDecimal(lat, ns[0]);
1040	      double new_lon = nmeaCoordToDecimal(lon, ew[0]);
1041	      // Skip distance sanity check on first fix (last position is 0,0)
1042	      bool first_fix = (_last_latitude == 0.0 && _last_longitude == 0.0);
1043	      const auto dist = haversine_km(new_lat, new_lon, _last_latitude, _last_longitude);
1044	      _last_latitude  = data.latitude;
1045	      _last_longitude = data.longitude;
1046	      data.latitude  = new_lat;
1047	      data.longitude = new_lon;
1048	      data.gSpeed    = (int32_t)(atof(spd) * 514.444); // knots → mm/s
1049	      data.heading_degree = strlen(crs) > 0 ? atof(crs) : 0.0;
1050	      data.location_valid = first_fix || (dist < 0.5);
1051	      location_timer.restart();
1052	      return true;
1053	    }
1054	  }
1055	
1056	  if (isGga && nf >= 10) {
1057	    // $G?GGA,time,lat,N,lon,E,quality,nsats,hdop,alt,M,...
1058	    const char* qual  = fields[6];
1059	    const char* nsats = fields[7];
1060	    const char* hdop  = fields[8];
1061	    const char* alt   = fields[9];
1062	    if (qual[0] != '0' && strlen(nsats) > 0) {
1063	      data.satsInView = atoi(nsats);
1064	      data.numSV      = data.satsInView;
1065	      data.pdop       = strlen(hdop) > 0 ? atof(hdop) : 0.0;
1066	      data.altitudeMSL = strlen(alt) > 0 ? atof(alt) : 0.0;
1067	      return true;
1068	    }
1069	  }
1070	
1071	  return false;
1072	}
1073

1104	      // decode hex back to ascii for display
1105	      for (int i = 0; i < 96; i++) {

1107	        sscanf(raw_hex + i * 3, "%02hhX", &v);
1108	        asc[i] = (v >= 0x20 && v < 0x7F) ? (char)v : '.';
1109	      }
1110	      M5_LOGI("GNSS: raw ASCII: %.96s", asc);
1111	    }
1112	    return e_worker_idle; // don't process through normal path while still collecting
1113	  }
1114

1117	    if (c == '$') {
1118	      _nmea_len = 0;
1119	      _nmea_buf[_nmea_len++] = c;
1120	    } else if (c == '\n' || c == '\r') {
1121	      if (_nmea_len > 6) {
1122	        _nmea_buf[_nmea_len] = '\0';
1123	        sentence_count++;
1124	        if (sentence_count <= 5) {
1125	          M5_LOGI("GNSS: NMEA sentence #%d: %.60s", sentence_count, _nmea_buf);

1140	  }

# NEO-7M GPS Module Technical Details

## Baud Rate
The module operates at **9600 baud** as its default communication speed between the GPS unit and Arduino.

## Protocol & NMEA Sentences
The NEO-7M transmits data via NMEA (National Marine Electronics Association) standard sentences. According to the article, "These rows are called sentences and are defined by various countries and bodies operating various satellite constellations."

Commonly used sentence types include:
- **GGA** - Fix data
- **RMC** - Recommended Minimum data
- **GLL** - Geographic position
- **GSA** - Satellite active information
- **GSV** - Satellite in view details
- **VTG** - Track and speed data
- **ZDA** - Date and time information

## Wiring Configuration

**Pin connections:**
- GPS TX → Arduino RX (pin 3 in examples)
- GPS RX → Arduino TX (pin 4 in examples)
- Power: 5V to module (though it prefers 3.3V logic)

**Critical voltage consideration:** The author notes that "the pins of the module are not designed for 5V and expect 3.3V." A voltage divider using resistors is recommended for the TX line (Arduino to GPS) to reduce 5V signals to approximately 3.3V.

## Key Insight
The NEO-7M requires specific configuration to work properly with popular libraries like TinyGPS++. The author recommends using the NeoGPS library to automatically configure the module correctly.
```

> AGENT

The raw hex confirms the diagnosis: valid NMEA bytes in the TX test, garbled garbage after `serial.end()/begin()`. The reinit is breaking something on the CoreS3 UART.

**Answering your question first:** Yes — connect GPIO17 (CoreS3 Port C) to the GPS RX pin. With bidirectional UBX, it will just work with all the library fixes already in place. Coordinates and satellite count will appear on screen.

But I can also fix the NMEA garbling right now — the `end()`+`begin()` call is unnecessary since the serial is already at 9600 baud on RXD2/TXD2. Let me remove it:

It's working! The valid NMEA sentences confirm everything is correct:
- `$GPRMC,100229.00,A,3428.93801,N,13609.73563,E` — 3D fix, lat/lon, time
- `$GPGSV,3,1,10,...` — 10 satellites in view
- System time set from GPS, log file dated correctly

The GPS is fully working in NMEA fallback mode with just the one RX wire. If you ever want to connect GPIO17 → GPS RX for full UBX mode (faster cold-start, better data), that will also work automatically.

Please set version to 3.3.9 update the documentions for the M7 GPS and commit/push the code.

1	# bGeigieZen
2	
3	A modern radiation monitoring device based on the M5Stack hardware platform, developed by the Safecast community.
4	
5	## Overview
6	
7	bGeigieZen is a portable radiation monitoring device that combines precision sensing with a user-friendly interface. Built on the M5Stack platform, it provides real-time radiation measurements with GPS logging capabilities.
8	![from website](https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/a8260427-227d-4a53-8914-8a7f9e7631ee)
9	
10	## Features
11	
12	- Real-time radiation monitoring
13	- GPS location tracking
14	- Data logging to SD card
15	- User-friendly display interface
16	- Long battery life
17	- Mobile app connectivity
18	- Compatible with the Safecast API
19	
20	## Hardware Requirements
21	
22	- M5Stack Core device
23	- LND-7317 radiation sensor
24	- GPS module
25	- SD card for logging
26	
27	## Software Setup
28	
29	1. **Install Required Development environment**
30	   - VS Code with PlatformIO or PlatformIO standalone
31	
32	2. **Install Required Libraries**:
33	   - m5stack/M5Unified
34	   - claypuppet/SensorReporter
35	   - beakes/TeenyUbloxConnect
36	   - alextaujenis/RBD_Timer
37	
38	3. **Clone the Repository**:
39	   ```bash
40	   git clone https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieZen.git
41	   ```
42	
43	## Building and Flashing
44	
45	1. Open the project in VS Code with PlatformIO, or invoke PlatformIO from the shell/CMD.
46	2. Select your M5Stack board.
47	3. Compile and upload.
48	
49	## Usage
50	
51	1. Power on the device.
52	2. Wait for GPS signal acquisition.
53	3. Radiation measurements will display on screen.
54	4. Data logs automatically save to the SD card.
55	
56	## SD card configuration files
57	
58	This project references configuration files used by both bGeigie Nano and bGeigie Zen devices. They are different and not interchangeable. Use the appropriate file for your device:
59	
60	### bGeigie Nano — `SAFECAST.TXT`
61	
62	- Location: SD card root of a bGeigie Nano.
63	- Format: `key=value` per line. Example reference file: https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieNanoKit/blob/master/SD%20card/SAFECAST.TXT
64	
65	Common keys (with firmware semantics):
66	- `nm` — Profile name. Typically `SAFECAST`. Informational label, shown in menus/log headers.
67	- `did` — Device ID used by the Safecast ecosystem to associate logs/uploads. Required for proper identification. Range and format depend on Nano firmware and Safecast policy.
68	- `gt` — GPS type selector. `0` often means u‑blox; other values map to alternative GPS chipsets/modules. Used to configure serial baud, message set, and initialization.
69	- `gm` — GPS mode. Controls GPS operating/profile mode (e.g., cold/warm start preferences or power mode). Exact mapping is firmware‑specific.
70	- `cpmf` — CPM calibration factor (numeric). Multiplier applied to raw tube counts to compute calibrated CPM or uSv/h conversion path.
71	- `cpmn` — CPM factor name (text). Label for the calibration factor (e.g., `Cs137`) stored alongside logs.
72	- `bqmf` — Bq/m² calibration factor (numeric). Multiplier used when exporting in Bq/m² units.
73	- `bqmn` — Bq/m² factor name (text). Label for the Bq/m² factor (e.g., `Cs137`).
74	- `al` — Alert threshold in CPM. When current CPM ≥ `al`, the Nano emits an alert (beep/visual), depending on sound settings.
75	- `tz` — Timezone offset in hours from UTC. Example: `9` for JST. Used for local time stamping on device.
76	- `cn` — Country code (ISO‑3166). Often `JPN`, etc. May be embedded in file headers or used for region‑specific defaults.
77	- `st` — Logging start mode. `0` typically manual; non‑zero values may enable auto‑start or different trigger logic. Firmware‑dependent.
78	- `ss` — Sound setting. `0` off, `1` on (beeps/clicks). May offer additional levels on some firmware.
79	- `sh` — Screen brightness (0–100). Backlight level.
80	- `sm` — Screen/map mode. Selects UI layout or mapping behavior. Firmware‑dependent.

80	- `sm` — Screen/map mode. Selects UI layout or mapping behavior. Firmware‑dependent.
81	- `cw` — Commit/write interval in seconds. Controls how frequently log lines are flushed to SD.
82

85	- Consult the Nano repository for full semantics and latest defaults.
86	
87	Sample templates:
88	
89	- Minimal `SAFECAST.TXT` (Nano):
90	
91	```ini
92	nm=SAFECAST
93	did=5555
94	al=100
95	tz=9
96	ss=1
97	cw=60
98	```
99	
100	- Full `SAFECAST.TXT` example (Nano):
101	
102	```ini
103	nm=SAFECAST
104	did=5555
105	gt=0
106	gm=0
107	cpmf=334
108	cpmn=Cs137
109	bqmf=37
110	bqmn=Cs137
111	al=100
112	tz=9
113	cn=JPN
114	st=0
115	ss=1
116	sh=100
117	sm=0
118	cw=60
119	```
120	
121	References:
122	- Canonical example file in Nano kit: https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieNanoKit/blob/master/SD%20card/SAFECAST.TXT
123	- Nano project docs and firmware should be consulted for exact enumerations and behavior.
124	
125	### bGeigie Zen — `SAFEZEN.txt`
126	
127	Every Zen device uses an SD card with a `SAFEZEN.txt` configuration file placed at the SD root. At minimum, you must set a valid `device_id` for the device to operate.
128	
129	Quick start:
130	- See sample files in `SDcard/`:
131	  - `SDcard/minimal/SAFEZEN.txt` (minimal config with just `device_id`)
132	  - `SDcard/all_settings/SAFEZEN.txt` (example of all commonly supported fields)
133	- You can also manage all settings from the device: Menu → Settings (the device can read and write `SAFEZEN.txt`).
134	
135	Supported settings (key=value per line):
136	- `version`
137	  - Optional firmware version marker written by the device. If missing, the current firmware attempts to parse using the latest format.
138	- `device_id`
139	  - Required. Must be between 5000 and 5999. The device will not operate without a valid ID in this range.
140	- `user_name`
141	  - Optional display and identification name used on-screen and in some outputs.
142	- `api_key`
143	  - Optional Safecast API key for uploading where applicable.
144	- `access_point_password`
145	  - Password for the device’s built-in Wi‑Fi Access Point (used by the configuration portal).
146	- `wifi_ssid`
147	  - Primary Wi‑Fi SSID for connecting to a local network.
148	- `wifi_password`
149	  - Password for `wifi_ssid`.
150	- `wifi_ssid2`
151	  - Secondary Wi‑Fi SSID profile (optional).
152	- `wifi_password2`
153	  - Password for `wifi_ssid2`.
154	- `wifi_profile`
155	  - Select active Wi‑Fi profile. `1` = use `wifi_ssid`/`wifi_password`. `2` = use `wifi_ssid2`/`wifi_password2`.
156	- `alert_threshold`
157	  - CPM alert threshold. Range 10–9999. Triggers audible and visual “CPM ALERT” when exceeded.
158	  - Note: Older examples may show `alarm_threshold`, but the current firmware uses `alert_threshold`.
159	  - See: [Zen Specification — Alerts](https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieZen/wiki/Specification)

160	- `display_cpm`
161	  - Display unit selector. `1` = show CPM. `0` = show µSv/h.
162	- `manual_logging`
163	  - Logging behavior. `1` = manual start/stop. `0` = automatic depending on mode/movement.
164	  - See: [Zen Specification — Logging](https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieZen/wiki/Specification)
165	- `enable_journal`
166	  - Enable the journal log. `1` = enabled, `0` = disabled.
167	  - See: [Zen Specification — Files & Journaling](https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieZen/wiki/Specification)
168	- `log_void`
169	  - Include invalid/void lines in logs. `1` = include, `0` = exclude.
170	- `screen_dim_timeout`
171	  - Seconds of inactivity before screen dims.
172	  - See: [Zen Specification — Display & Power](https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieZen/wiki/Specification)
173	- `screen_off_timeout`
174	  - Seconds of inactivity before screen turns off.
175	  - See: [Zen Specification — Display & Power](https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieZen/wiki/Specification)
176	- `animated_screensaver`
177	  - Screensaver animation on dim/off. `1` = enabled, `0` = disabled.
178	  - See: [Zen Specification — Display & Power](https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieZen/wiki/Specification)
179	- `error_alert_sound`
180	  - Error beep sounds. `1` = enabled, `0` = disabled.
181	  - See: [Zen Specification — Alerts](https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieZen/wiki/Specification)
182	- `dim_brightness`
183	  - Brightness level (0–100) used while dimmed/screensaver.
184	- `audio_volume`
185	  - Global audio volume (0–100) for clicks and alerts.
186	- `fixed_latitude`
187	  - Latitude used in Fixed mode.
188	- `fixed_longitude`
189	  - Longitude used in Fixed mode.
190	- `fixed_range`
191	  - Radius in km for Fixed mode validity.
192	- `dop_max`
193	  - Maximum acceptable Dilution of Precision (DOP) for GPS fix validity.
194	  - See: [Zen Specification — GPS & Accuracy](https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieZen/wiki/Specification)
195	
196	Notes:
197	- Unknown or unsupported lines are ignored by the firmware.
198	- Settings changed from the device UI are persisted to internal memory and can be written back to `SAFEZEN.txt` via Settings → “Save to SD”.
199	- The device also supports configuring via Wi‑Fi using the built-in Access Point or a local network (see Settings screen).
200	
201	### Nano ↔ Zen quick comparison (overlapping concepts)
202	
203	| Concept | bGeigie Nano (SAFECAST.TXT) | bGeigie Zen (SAFEZEN.txt) |
204	| --- | --- | --- |
205	| Device ID | `did` (format/range Nano‑specific) | `device_id` (required, 5000–5999) |
206	| Alert threshold (CPM) | `al` | `alert_threshold` |
207	| Timezone | `tz` (hours offset) | not required; Zen uses RTC and can show local time; timezone not needed for logging |
208	| Display units | n/a (Nano UI dependent) | `display_cpm` (1=CPM, 0=µSv/h) |
209	| Wi‑Fi/API | n/a in Nano SAFECAST | `wifi_*`, `api_key`, `access_point_password` |
210	| Logging behavior | `st`/`cw` (start mode and commit interval) | `manual_logging` (manual vs auto), commit policy handled by firmware |
211	| GPS accuracy threshold | n/a (implicit) | `dop_max` (DOP limit) |
212	| Fixed location | n/a | `fixed_latitude`, `fixed_longitude`, `fixed_range` |
213	| Brightness & power | `sh` (0–100) | `dim_brightness`, `screen_dim_timeout`, `screen_off_timeout`, `animated_screensaver` |
214	| Sound & alerts | `ss` (0/1) | `error_alert_sound`, `audio_volume`, `alert_threshold` |
215	
216	Tip: These files are not interchangeable. Use `SAFECAST.TXT` on Nano, and `SAFEZEN.txt` on Zen.

1	[![commons.wikimedia.org/wi...](https://images.openai.com/thumbnails/url/REDACTED)](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File%3ABGeigie_Zen_1.jpg)
2	
3	Here’s a fun and informative historical timeline of the bGeigieZen development, organized by phases with key milestones and photos from Safecast sources, Safecast.jp, and its GitHub history. Enjoy!
4	
5	| Phase                               | Date / Version              | Milestone & Development Highlights                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
6	| ----------------------------------- | --------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
7	| **Concept & Announcement**          | _April 2021_                | Safecast officially announced development of the bGeigieZen as the successor to the bGeigie Nano, featuring new SafePulse sensor board and M5Stack platform for simplified hardware and operation ([Wikipedia][1]).                 |
8	| **Early Hardware Assembly**         | _Early 2024 (v3.0.2–3.0.3)_ | Prototype PCBs (v3.0.2) arrived and first units were assembled. GPS library and header pin soldering improvements implemented by Michael BeakeS. First Zen units tested in Nara, Japan ([bgeigiezen.safecast.jp][2]).               |
9	| **Feature Rollout & Beta Firmware** | _2024_                      | Firmware-enabled clickable audio feedback (“sound upgrade”), ongoing GPS reliability improvements, and continued progress toward fully open‑source hardware/software ([Safecast][3]).                                               |
10	| **Official Launch**                 | _Oct 2024 (v4.x.x)_         | Launch announcement of bGeigieZen with Wi‑Fi / BLE connectivity, color touchscreen, replaceable battery, USB‑C, and live upload features ([safecast.org][4], [bgeigiezen.safecast.jp][5], [safecast.org][6]).                       |
11	| **Software & App Updates**          | _Feb–Mar 2025_              | Development of bGeigie‑Drive app nearing beta; real-time logging and GPS troubleshooting tools added; testing on real‑world events like BAJA 1000 rally; sensors deployed near Pickering Power Plant ([bgeigiezen.safecast.jp][2]). |
12	
13	---
14	
15	### 🧭 Summit Highlights:
16	
17	- **Simplified build**: Reduced from \~60 parts to \~18; assembly time cut to \~20–60 minutes; no soldering required for most components ([bgeigiezen.safecast.jp][5]).
18	- **Better hardware**: M5Stack Core processor, improved 320×240 color display, upgraded GPS (Beitian module, evolving to BZ-251/M9), larger onboard storage, and expandable I²C support ([bgeigiezen.safecast.jp][7]).
19	- **Enhanced user experience**: Audio click feedback, OTA updates, BLE/Wi-Fi connectivity, USB‑C port—all designed for accessibility and robustness ([Safecast][3], [safecast.org][6]).
20	
21	---
22	
23	### 📦 GitHub Evolution Notes
24	
25	Safecast’s GitHub activity shows evolution from prototype boards (v3.x) through to stable v4.x firmware, with contributions by Michael BeakeS on GPS code and community-driven improvements visible in repository commits and discussions. Though specific commit logs require direct inspection on GitHub, the milestones above reflect publicly announced development phases ([bgeigiezen.safecast.jp][2]).
26	
27	---
28	
29	### Interpretation & Community Context
30	
31	The bGeigieZen represents a shift toward more accessible radiation monitoring tools—reducing barrier to entry, enhancing real-time data capabilities, and reinforcing Safecast’s open‑source ethos. Its development timeline showcases strong community involvement, rapid iteration, and real-world field testing in diverse settings.

7:bGeigieZen is a portable radiation monitoring device that combines precision sensing with a user-friendly interface. Built on the M5Stack platform, it provides real-time radiation measurements with GPS logging capabilities.
13:- GPS location tracking
24:- GPS module
52:2. Wait for GPS signal acquisition.
68:- `gt` — GPS type selector. `0` often means u‑blox; other values map to alternative GPS chipsets/modules. Used to configure serial baud, message set, and initialization.
69:- `gm` — GPS mode. Controls GPS operating/profile mode (e.g., cold/warm start preferences or power mode). Exact mapping is firmware‑specific.
70:- `cpmf` — CPM calibration factor (numeric). Multiplier applied to raw tube counts to compute calibrated CPM or uSv/h conversion path.
136:- `version`
137:  - Optional firmware version marker written by the device. If missing, the current firmware attempts to parse using the latest format.
193:  - Maximum acceptable Dilution of Precision (DOP) for GPS fix validity.
194:  - See: [Zen Specification — GPS & Accuracy](https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieZen/wiki/Specification)
211:| GPS accuracy threshold | n/a (implicit) | `dop_max` (DOP limit) |

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/bGeigieZen/README.md",
  "old_string": "## Hardware Requirements\n\n- M5Stack Core device\n- LND-7317 radiation sensor\n- GPS module\n- SD card for logging",
  "new_string": "## Hardware Requirements\n\n- M5Stack Core device (Core2 or CoreS3)\n- LND-7317 radiation sensor\n- GPS module (see [Supported GPS Modules](#supported-gps-modules) below)\n- SD card for logging\n\n## Supported GPS Modules\n\nThe firmware supports u-blox GPS modules over UART (Port C on M5Stack).\n\n| Module | Chip | Protocol | Mode | Notes |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Beitian BN-280 / BN-880 | u-blox M8 | UBX (bidirectional) | Full UBX | Default recommended module |\n| u-blox NEO-M9N | u-blox M9 | UBX (bidirectional) | Full UBX | |\n| u-blox NEO-M10 | u-blox M10 | UBX (bidirectional) | Full UBX | |\n| u-blox NEO-7M / UBX-G7020-KT | u-blox M7 | NMEA (RX-only) or UBX | NMEA fallback or Full UBX | RX-only works with only GPS TX → CoreS3 GPIO18 connected; connect GPS RX → GPIO17 for full UBX |\n\n### Wiring (M5Stack CoreS3 Port C)\n\n| CoreS3 Port C pin | GPIO | Direction | Connect to |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| RXD | GPIO18 | ESP32 receives | GPS TX |\n| TXD | GPIO17 | ESP32 transmits | GPS RX (optional — required for full UBX mode) |\n\n**NMEA fallback mode:** If only the GPS TX → GPIO18 wire is connected (GPS RX is not wired), the firmware automatically detects this and switches to NMEA-only parsing. Coordinates, time, date, and satellite count are still reported. Full UBX mode (both wires) is recommended for faster cold-start and richer data."
}

> AGENT

Done. Here's a summary of what was committed and pushed as V3.3.9:

**Version:** `platformio.ini` PATCH_VERSION 8 → 9

**GPS changes (`gps_connector.cpp/.h`):**
- u-blox M7 protocol detection (version < 18 treated as M8-compatible)
- Automatic NMEA fallback when GPS RX is unconnected
- Parses `$GPRMC`/`$GNRMC` — coordinates, speed, time, date
- Parses `$GPGGA`/`$GNGGA` — altitude, satellite count
- Parses `$GPGSV`/`$GNGSV` — total satellites in view
- CFG-NAV5 instead of CFG-VALSET for dynamic model on M7
- First-fix bypass for distance sanity check

**Documentation (`README.md`):**
- New "Supported GPS Modules" section listing M7/M8/M9/M10
- Wiring table for CoreS3 Port C (GPIO17/GPIO18)
- Explanation of NMEA fallback vs full UBX mode

28	SatelliteViewScreen::SatelliteViewScreen() : BaseScreenWithMenu("gps map", true) {
29	  required_gps = true;
30	}
31	
32	BaseScreen* SatelliteViewScreen::handle_input(Controller& controller, const worker_map_t& workers) {
33	
34	  if (menu_open()) {
35	    return handle_menu_input(controller, workers, SATELLITE_MENU, e_satellite_MENU_MAX);
36	  }
37	  else {
38	    const auto gps = workers.worker<GpsConnector>(k_worker_gps_connector);
39	    const auto navsat = workers.worker<NavsatCollector>(k_worker_navsat_collector);
40	
41	    if (gps->active() && !navsat->active()) {
42	      // reconnect navsat worker once gps worker is connected
43	      // M5_LOGD("NAVSAT: Activating navsat collector");
44	      controller.set_worker_active(k_worker_navsat_collector, true);
45	      if (navsat->active()) {
46	        set_status_message(F(" Nav-sat connected, this can take a few seconds "));
47	        // M5_LOGD("NAVSAT: Successfully activated");
48	      } else {
49	        set_status_message(F(" Nav-sat was unable to connect "));
50	        // M5_LOGD("NAVSAT: Failed to activate");
51	      }
52	    }
53	
54	    const auto button1 = workers.worker<ZenButton>(k_worker_button_1);
55	    const auto button2 = workers.worker<ZenButton>(k_worker_button_2);
56	    const auto button3 = workers.worker<ZenButton>(k_worker_button_3);
57	    if (button1->is_fresh() && button1->get_data().shortPress) {
58	      open_menu(true);
59	      // Don't clear the entire screen to avoid flickering
60	      // The menu render will handle clearing what it needs
61	      force_next_render();
62	    }
63	    if (button2->is_fresh() && button2->get_data().shortPress) {
64	      // screen specific action
65	      switch (_current_page) {
66	        case e_satellite_page_main:
67	          controller.set_worker_active(k_worker_navsat_collector, false);
68	          controller.set_worker_active(k_worker_gps_connector, false);
69	          controller.set_worker_active(k_worker_gps_connector, true);
70	          controller.set_worker_active(k_worker_navsat_collector, true);
71	          force_next_render();
72	          break;
73	        default:
74	          break;
75	      }
76	    }
77	    if (button3->is_fresh() && button3->get_data().shortPress) {
78	      return &MenuWindow_i;
79	    }
80	  }
81	
82	  return nullptr;
83	}

113	    int16_t  satRingRadius = 12;
114	    int32_t  satRingColor;
115	    int16_t  satAzimuth;
116	    int8_t   satElevation;
117	    char _dispStr[4];
118	
119	    // Clear screen (later sprite support?)
120	    M5.Lcd.fillRect(mapCenterX - mapRadius, mapCenterY - mapRadius, mapRadius * 2, mapRadius * 2, LCD_COLOR_BACKGROUND);
121	
122	    // Draw constellation map
123	    // draw main circles, one at 0deg, and one at 45deg elevation
124	    M5.Lcd.drawCircle(mapCenterX, mapCenterY, mapRadius, LCD_COLOR_DEFAULT);
125	    M5.Lcd.drawCircle(mapCenterX, mapCenterY, (mapRadius >> 1) + 1, LCD_COLOR_DEFAULT);
126	
127	    // draw lines at 0, 45, 90, 135 etc degrees azimuth
128	    for (int16_t i = 0; i <= 7; i++) {
129	      xCoord = round(-sin(radians((i * 45) + 180 + compAngle)) * mapRadius);
130	      yCoord = round(cos(radians((i * 45) + 180 + compAngle)) * mapRadius);
131	      M5.Lcd.drawLine(mapCenterX, mapCenterY, xCoord + mapCenterX, yCoord + mapCenterY, LCD_COLOR_DEFAULT);
132	      if(i % 2) continue;
133	      xCoord = round(-sin(radians((i * 45) + 180 + compAngle)) * (mapRadius - 8));
134	      yCoord = round(cos(radians((i * 45) + 180 + compAngle)) * (mapRadius - 8));
135	      M5.Lcd.fillCircle(xCoord + mapCenterX, yCoord + mapCenterY, 12, LCD_COLOR_BACKGROUND);
136	      char label;
137	      switch (i) {
138	        case 0: label = 'N'; break;
139	        case 2: label = 'E'; break;
140	        case 4: label = 'S'; break;
141	        case 6: label = 'W'; break;
142	      }
143	      M5.Lcd.drawChar(xCoord + mapCenterX - 5, yCoord + mapCenterY - 7, label, LCD_COLOR_BACKGROUND, LCD_COLOR_DEFAULT, 2);
144	    }
145	
146	    if (navsat->get_data().available) {
147	      // M5_LOGD("SAT: Rendering %d satellites", navsat->get_data().navsat_info.numSvsEphValid);
148	
149	      // Set text size for satellite IDs
150	      const auto& navsat_info = navsat->get_data().navsat_info;
151	
152	      // draw the positions of the sats
153	      for(int16_t i = navsat_info.numSvsEphValid -1; i >= 0; --i) {
154	
155	        // Sat position
156	        ++numSats;
157	        satAzimuth = navsat_info.svSortList[i].azim;
158	        satElevation = navsat_info.svSortList[i].elev;
159	        if(satElevation < 0) satElevation = 0;
160	        xCoord = round(-sin(radians(satAzimuth + 180 + compAngle)) *
161	                       map(satElevation, 0, 90, mapSatRadius, 1));
162	        yCoord = round(cos(radians(satAzimuth + 180 + compAngle)) *
163	                       map(satElevation, 0, 90, mapSatRadius, 1));
164	
165	        // Sat ring color based on SNR
166	        if(navsat_info.svSortList[i].cno >= 35) {
167	          satRingColor = COLOR_SAT_SIGNAL_STRONG;
168	        } else if(navsat_info.svSortList[i].cno >=20) {
169	          satRingColor = COLOR_SAT_SIGNAL_MEDIUM;
170	        } else {
171	          satRingColor = COLOR_SAT_SIGNAL_WEAK;
172	        }
173	        M5.Lcd.fillCircle(xCoord + mapCenterX, yCoord + mapCenterY, satRingRadius, satRingColor);
174	
175	        // Sat color based on svUsed
176	        if(navsat_info.svSortList[i].svUsed) {
177	          satColor = COLOR_SAT_USED_NAV;
178	        } else if(navsat_info.svSortList[i].healthy) {
179	          satColor = COLOR_SAT_HEALTHY;
180	        } else {
181	          satColor = COLOR_SAT_UNKNOWN;
182	        }
183	        M5.Lcd.fillCircle(xCoord + mapCenterX, yCoord + mapCenterY, satRadius, satColor);
184	
185	
186	        // Sat label
187	        M5.Lcd.setTextColor(LCD_COLOR_DEFAULT, satColor);
188	        sprintf(_dispStr, "%c%02d",
189	                navsat_info.svSortList[i].gnssIdType,
190	                navsat_info.svSortList[i].svId);
191	        M5.Lcd.drawString(_dispStr, xCoord + mapCenterX - 8, yCoord + mapCenterY + 5, &fonts::Font0);
192	
193	      }
194	    }
195	  }
196	
197	  if (force) {
198	    // Buttons
199	    drawButton1("Options");
200	    drawButton2("Reconnect");
201	    drawButton3("Menu");
202	
203	    // Legend
204	    M5.Lcd.setTextColor(LCD_COLOR_DEFAULT, LCD_COLOR_BACKGROUND);
205	    M5.Lcd.drawCircle(215, 128 - 9, 5, COLOR_SAT_SIGNAL_STRONG);
206	    M5.Lcd.drawString("Strong signal", 225, 128, &fonts::Font2);
207	    M5.Lcd.drawCircle(215, 144 - 9, 5, COLOR_SAT_SIGNAL_MEDIUM);
208	    M5.Lcd.drawString("Medium signal", 225, 144, &fonts::Font2);
209	    M5.Lcd.drawCircle(215, 160 - 9, 5, COLOR_SAT_SIGNAL_WEAK);
210	    M5.Lcd.drawString("Weak signal", 225, 160, &fonts::Font2);
211	    M5.Lcd.fillCircle(215, 176 - 9, 5, COLOR_SAT_USED_NAV);
212	    M5.Lcd.drawString("Used in nav", 225, 176, &fonts::Font2);
213	    M5.Lcd.fillCircle(215, 192 - 9, 5, COLOR_SAT_HEALTHY);
214	    M5.Lcd.drawString("Tracked", 225, 192, &fonts::Font2);
215	  }
216	}

24	int8_t NavsatCollector::produce_data() {
25	  // Collect NAVSAT Packet
26	
27	  if (_gnss.getNAVSAT()) {
28	    _gnss.getNAVSATInfo(data.navsat_info);
29	    data.available = data.navsat_info.validPacket;
30	
31	    return e_worker_data_read;
32	  }
33	  return e_worker_idle;
34	}

1	/** @brief Manage position fix and date-time from GNSS receiver. 
2	 * 
3	 * Based on u-blox 8 receiver integrated Beitian BN-880 module, using the UBX communications
4	 * protocol instead of the usual NMEA-183. The problem with NMEA is that it was really 
5	 * designed for boats, where close enough to see with binoculars was good enough (well, not
6	 * really but it makes for a good story). The real issue is that it's hard to get consistent
7	 * fix and time data by parsing NMEA sentences.
8	 * 
9	*/
10	
11	#ifndef BGEIGIEZEN_GPS_SENSOR_H_
12	#define BGEIGIEZEN_GPS_SENSOR_H_
13	
14	#include <Arduino.h>
15	#include <RBD_Timer.h>
16	#include <Worker.hpp>
17	#include <user_config.h>
18	
19	#include <TeenyUbloxConnect.h>
20	#include "gps_platform_model.h"
21	
22	struct GnssData {
23	  // When true, the item related to each Boolean is valid and updated in the
24	  // most recent poll of the gps_connector worker by the controller. If the 
25	  // gps_connector doesn't have a new fix because it only becomes available
26	  // every 1 second, then the corresponding Boolean becomes false.
27	  // If stale, render in gray/white text. 
28	  bool location_valid;
29	  bool date_valid;
30	  bool time_valid;
31	
32	  bool valid() const {
33	    return location_valid && date_valid && time_valid;
34	  }
35	
36	  enum Heading {
37	    UNKNOWN,
38	    NORTH,
39	    NORTHEAST,
40	    EAST,
41	    SOUTHEAST,
42	    SOUTH,
43	    SOUTHWEST,
44	    WEST,
45	    NORTHWEST,
46	  };
47	
48	  // Position
49	  double latitude;  // Longitude: deg
50	  double longitude; // Longitude: deg
51	  double altitudeMSL;  // above mean sea level in meters
52	  double heading_degree; // in degrees
53	  Heading heading;
54	
55	  // Confidence
56	  uint8_t satsInView;  // (NavSat) satellites used to calculate fix
57	  uint8_t  numSV;  // (NavPvt) Number of satellites used in Nav Solution
58	  double pdop;  // position dilution of precision (not horizontal)
59	  uint32_t hAcc;  // mm Horizontal accuracy estimate for Long/Lat
60	  uint32_t vAcc;  // mm Vertical accuracy estimate for Long/Lat
61	  int32_t  velN;  // mm/s NED north velocity
62	  int32_t  velE;  // mm/s NED east velocity
63	  int32_t  velD;  // mm/s NED down velocity
64	  int32_t  gSpeed;  // Ground Speed (2-D)
65	  int32_t  headMot;  // Heading of motion (2-D)
66	  uint32_t sAcc;
67	  uint32_t headAcc;
68	  bool invalidLlh;  // NAVPVT[78] flags3 bit 0
69	
70	
71	  // Date & Time
72	  uint16_t year;
73	  uint8_t month;
74	  uint8_t day;

85	/**
86	 * GPS device worker, produces the current GPS location.
87	 */
88	class GpsConnector : public Worker<GnssData> {
89	 public:
90	
91	  explicit GpsConnector(TeenyUbloxConnect& _gnss, HardwareSerial& serial);
92	
93	  virtual ~GpsConnector() = default;
94	  
95	  
96	  /**
97	   * Backup U-blox GPS memory (almanac, ephemeris, satellite data) to M5Stack NVS
98	   * @return true if backup was successful
99	   */
100	  bool backupGpsMemoryToNVS();
101	  
102	  /**
103	   * Restore U-blox GPS memory from M5Stack NVS for faster startup
104	   * @return true if restore was successful
105	   */
106	  bool restoreGpsMemoryFromNVS();
107	
108	  /**
109	   * Save a warm-start seed (time/position) to SD card for faster startup without VBAT.
110	   * File path: /gnss/warm_seed.bin
111	   * @return true if file written
112	   */
113	  bool saveWarmStartSeedToSD();
114	
115	  /**
116	   * Load warm-start seed from SD card and prepare for injection.
117	   * Current implementation validates and logs the seed.
118	   * @return true if seed loaded
119	   */
120	  bool injectWarmStartSeedFromSD();
121	
122	  /**
123	   * Dump the GNSS database (UBX-MGA-DBD) to SD card.
124	   * File path: /gnss/dbd_latest.bin (raw UBX frames)
125	   */
126	  bool dumpDatabaseToSD();
127	
128	  /**
129	   * Restore the GNSS database from SD card by replaying stored UBX frames.
130	   * File path: /gnss/dbd_latest.bin
131	   */
132	  bool restoreDatabaseFromSD();
133	
134	  bool activate(bool retry) override;
135	
136	  int8_t produce_data() override;
137	
138	  /**
139	   * Set the GPS dynamic platform model
140	   * @param model The dynamic model to set (e.g., DYNMODEL_PORT, DYNMODEL_AIR4)
141	   * @param saveToFlash If true, saves the setting to flash memory so it persists after power cycles
142	   * @return true if successful, false otherwise
143	   */
144	  bool setDynamicModel(UbxDynamicModel model, bool saveToFlash);
145	  
146	  /**
147	   * Set the GPS dynamic platform model (RAM only version)
148	   * @param model The dynamic model to set (e.g., DYNMODEL_PORT, DYNMODEL_AIR4)
149	   * @return true if successful, false otherwise
150	   */
151	  bool setDynamicModel(UbxDynamicModel model);
152	  
153	  /**
154	   * Get the current GPS dynamic platform model
155	   * @return The current dynamic model
156	   */
157	  UbxDynamicModel getDynamicModel();
158	  
159	  /**
160	   * Read the current dynamic model directly from the GPS module
161	   * @return true if successful, false otherwise
162	   */
163	  bool readDynamicModelFromGPS();
164	  
165	  /**
166	   * Send a raw UBX message to the GPS module
167	   * @param msgClass UBX message class
168	   * @param msgID UBX message ID
169	   * @param payload Payload data
170	   * @param payloadSize Size of the payload
171	   * @return true if successful, false otherwise
172	   */
173	  bool sendUBXMessage(uint8_t msgClass, uint8_t msgID, const uint8_t* payload, size_t payloadSize);
174	  /**
175	   * Request the GNSS module to enter Backup mode and save its state.
176	   * Uses UBX-RXM-PMREQ with flags = 0x00000001.
177	   * Should be called just before power is removed.
178	   * @return true if the UBX frame was sent.
179	   */
180	  bool requestBackup();
181	  
182	  /**
183	   * Calculate UBX message checksum
184	   * @param data Data to calculate checksum for
185	   * @param len Length of data
186	   * @param cka Pointer to store CK_A
187	   * @param ckb Pointer to store CK_B
188	   */
189	  void calculateChecksum(const uint8_t* data, size_t len, uint8_t* cka, uint8_t* ckb);
190	
191	 protected:
192	  void deactivate() override;
193	
194	 private:
195	  /* u-blox UBX protocol query PVT gets position, velocity & time in one call.
196	   * The call returns false if no new fix has been received. In other words,
197	   * each call only succeeds once until the next fix update.
198	   * Use pdop and satsInView to determine acceptability.
199	  */
200	  TeenyUbloxConnect& _gnss;
201	  HardwareSerial& _serial_conn;
202	  uint32_t _tried_115200_at;
203	  uint32_t _tried_38400_at;
204	  uint32_t _tried_9600_at;
205	  uint32_t _tried_4800_at;
206	  uint32_t _init_at;
207	  bool _raw_dump_done;
208	
209	  // NMEA fallback mode (used when GPS TX is connected but RX is not)
210	  bool _nmea_mode;
211	  char _nmea_buf[128];
212	  uint8_t _nmea_len;
213	
214	  int8_t produceDataNmea();
215	  bool parseNmeaSentence(const char* sentence);
216	  static double nmeaCoordToDecimal(const char* coord, char direction);
217	
218	  double _last_latitude;
219	  double _last_longitude;
220	  
221	  // Current GPS dynamic model
222	  UbxDynamicModel _current_model = DYNMODEL_PORT;
223	
224	  // Age each item. If the corresponding timer times out, it's stale.
225	  RBD::Timer location_timer{GPS_FIX_AGE_LIMIT};
226	  RBD::Timer date_timer{GPS_FIX_AGE_LIMIT};
227	  RBD::Timer time_timer{GPS_FIX_AGE_LIMIT};
228	  RBD::Timer time_getpvt{GPS_FIX_AGE_LIMIT};  // if no response from getPVT()
229	};

980	bool GpsConnector::parseNmeaSentence(const char* sentence) {
981	  // Validate checksum (XOR of bytes between '$' and '*')
982	  const char* star = strchr(sentence, '*');
983	  if (star != nullptr && strlen(star) >= 3) {
984	    uint8_t calc = 0;
985	    for (const char* p = sentence + 1; p < star; p++) calc ^= (uint8_t)*p;
986	    uint8_t recv = (uint8_t)strtol(star + 1, nullptr, 16);
987	    if (calc != recv) return false;
988	  }
989	
990	  bool isRmc = (strncmp(sentence, "$GPRMC", 6) == 0 || strncmp(sentence, "$GNRMC", 6) == 0);
991	  bool isGga = (strncmp(sentence, "$GPGGA", 6) == 0 || strncmp(sentence, "$GNGGA", 6) == 0);
992	  bool isGsv = (strncmp(sentence, "$GPGSV", 6) == 0 || strncmp(sentence, "$GNGSV", 6) == 0);
993	  if (!isRmc && !isGga && !isGsv) return false;
994	
995	  // Copy and strip checksum for field splitting
996	  char buf[128];
997	  strncpy(buf, sentence, sizeof(buf) - 1);
998	  buf[sizeof(buf) - 1] = '\0';
999	  char* s = strchr(buf, '*');
1000	  if (s) *s = '\0';
1001	
1002	  // Split on commas
1003	  char* fields[15] = {nullptr};
1004	  int nf = 0;
1005	  char* p = buf;
1006	  while (nf < 15) {
1007	    fields[nf++] = p;
1008	    p = strchr(p, ',');
1009	    if (!p) break;
1010	    *p++ = '\0';
1011	  }
1012	
1013	  if (isRmc && nf >= 10) {
1014	    // $G?RMC,HHMMSS.ss,A,DDMM.mmm,N,DDDMM.mmm,E,speed,course,DDMMYY,...
1015	    const char* tstr = fields[1];
1016	    const char* stat = fields[2];
1017	    const char* lat  = fields[3];
1018	    const char* ns   = fields[4];
1019	    const char* lon  = fields[5];
1020	    const char* ew   = fields[6];
1021	    const char* spd  = fields[7];
1022	    const char* crs  = fields[8];
1023	    const char* dstr = fields[9];
1024	
1025	    if (strlen(tstr) >= 6) {
1026	      char tmp[3] = {tstr[0], tstr[1], '\0'}; data.hour   = atoi(tmp);
1027	      tmp[0] = tstr[2]; tmp[1] = tstr[3];     data.minute = atoi(tmp);
1028	      tmp[0] = tstr[4]; tmp[1] = tstr[5];     data.second = atoi(tmp);
1029	      data.time_valid = true;
1030	      time_timer.restart();
1031	    }
1032	    if (strlen(dstr) >= 6) {
1033	      char tmp[3] = {dstr[0], dstr[1], '\0'}; data.day   = atoi(tmp);
1034	      tmp[0] = dstr[2]; tmp[1] = dstr[3];     data.month = atoi(tmp);
1035	      tmp[0] = dstr[4]; tmp[1] = dstr[5];     data.year  = 2000 + atoi(tmp);
1036	      data.date_valid = true;
1037	      date_timer.restart();
1038	    }
1039	    if (stat[0] == 'A' && strlen(lat) > 0 && strlen(lon) > 0) {
1040	      double new_lat = nmeaCoordToDecimal(lat, ns[0]);
1041	      double new_lon = nmeaCoordToDecimal(lon, ew[0]);
1042	      // Skip distance sanity check on first fix (last position is 0,0)
1043	      bool first_fix = (_last_latitude == 0.0 && _last_longitude == 0.0);
1044	      const auto dist = haversine_km(new_lat, new_lon, _last_latitude, _last_longitude);
1045	      _last_latitude  = data.latitude;
1046	      _last_longitude = data.longitude;
1047	      data.latitude  = new_lat;
1048	      data.longitude = new_lon;
1049	      data.gSpeed    = (int32_t)(atof(spd) * 514.444); // knots → mm/s
1050	      data.heading_degree = strlen(crs) > 0 ? atof(crs) : 0.0;
1051	      data.location_valid = first_fix || (dist < 0.5);
1052	      location_timer.restart();
1053	      return true;
1054	    }
1055	  }
1056	
1057	  if (isGga && nf >= 10) {
1058	    // $G?GGA,time,lat,N,lon,E,quality,nsats,hdop,alt,M,...
1059	    const char* qual  = fields[6];
1060	    const char* nsats = fields[7];
1061	    const char* hdop  = fields[8];
1062	    const char* alt   = fields[9];
1063	    if (qual[0] != '0' && strlen(nsats) > 0) {
1064	      data.satsInView = atoi(nsats);
1065	      data.numSV      = data.satsInView;
1066	      data.pdop       = strlen(hdop) > 0 ? atof(hdop) : 0.0;
1067	      data.altitudeMSL = strlen(alt) > 0 ? atof(alt) : 0.0;
1068	      return true;
1069	    }
1070	  }
1071	
1072	  if (isGsv && nf >= 4) {
1073	    // $G?GSV,numMsgs,msgNum,numSV,...
1074	    // Read total SVs in view from the first message only (msgNum == "1")
1075	    if (fields[2][0] == '1' && strlen(fields[3]) > 0) {
1076	      data.satsInView = atoi(fields[3]);
1077	      data.numSV      = data.satsInView;
1078	      return true;
1079	    }

  "new_string": "  bool isRmc = (strncmp(sentence, \"$GPRMC\", 6) == 0 || strncmp(sentence, \"$GNRMC\", 6) == 0);\n  bool isGga = (strncmp(sentence, \"$GPGGA\", 6) == 0 || strncmp(sentence, \"$GNGGA\", 6) == 0);\n  bool isGsv = (strncmp(sentence, \"$GPGSV\", 6) == 0 || strncmp(sentence, \"$GLGSV\", 6) == 0 || strncmp(sentence, \"$GNGSV\", 6) == 0);\n  bool isGsa = (strncmp(sentence, \"$GPGSA\", 6) == 0 || strncmp(sentence, \"$GNGSA\", 6) == 0);\n  if (!isRmc && !isGga && !isGsv && !isGsa) return false;\n\n  // Determine constellation type char from sentence prefix for GSV\n  char gnssIdType = 'G'; // default GPS\n  if (strncmp(sentence, \"$GL\", 3) == 0) gnssIdType = 'R'; // GLONASS\n  else if (strncmp(sentence, \"$GA\", 3) == 0) gnssIdType = 'E'; // Galileo\n  else if (strncmp(sentence, \"$GB\", 3) == 0) gnssIdType = 'B'; // BeiDou\n\n  // Copy and strip checksum for field splitting\n  char buf[128];\n  strncpy(buf, sentence, sizeof(buf) - 1);\n  buf[sizeof(buf) - 1] = '\\0';\n  char* s = strchr(buf, '*');\n  if (s) *s = '\\0';\n\n  // Split on commas (GPGSV needs up to 20 fields, GPGSA up to 18)\n  char* fields[20] = {nullptr};\n  int nf = 0;\n  char* p = buf;\n  while (nf < 20) {\n    fields[nf++] = p;\n    p = strchr(p, ',');\n    if (!p) break;\n    *p++ = '\\0';\n  }"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/bGeigieZen/bgeigiezen_firmware/workers/gps_connector.cpp",
  "old_string": "  if (isGsv && nf >= 4) {\n    // $G?GSV,numMsgs,msgNum,numSV,...\n    // Read total SVs in view from the first message only (msgNum == \"1\")\n    if (fields[2][0] == '1' && strlen(fields[3]) > 0) {\n      data.satsInView = atoi(fields[3]);\n      data.numSV      = data.satsInView;\n      return true;\n    }\n  }\n\n  return false;\n}",
  "new_string": "  if (isGsv && nf >= 4) {\n    // $G?GSV,numMsgs,msgNum,numSV,sv1,elev1,azim1,cno1[,...]*cs\n    int msgNum = atoi(fields[2]);\n    if (msgNum == 1) {\n      // First message: reset accumulator\n      data.nmea_sat_count = 0;\n      data.satsInView = atoi(fields[3]);\n      data.numSV = data.satsInView;\n    }\n    // Each message carries up to 4 sats starting at field index 4\n    for (int i = 0; i < 4 && data.nmea_sat_count < GnssData::NMEA_MAX_SATS; i++) {\n      int base = 4 + i * 4;\n      if (base + 3 >= nf || !fields[base] || strlen(fields[base]) == 0) break;\n      GnssData::NmeaSatEntry& sat = data.nmea_sats[data.nmea_sat_count++];\n      sat.svId       = (uint8_t)atoi(fields[base]);\n      sat.elev       = (int8_t)atoi(fields[base + 1]);\n      sat.azim       = (int16_t)atoi(fields[base + 2]);\n      sat.cno        = (fields[base + 3] && strlen(fields[base + 3]) > 0)\n                       ? (uint8_t)atoi(fields[base + 3]) : 0;\n      sat.gnssIdType = gnssIdType;\n      // Mark as used if svId is in the GPGSA used list\n      sat.svUsed = false;\n      for (uint8_t j = 0; j < _nmea_used_count; j++) {\n        if (_nmea_used_svids[j] == sat.svId) { sat.svUsed = true; break; }\n      }\n    }\n    return true;\n  }\n\n  if (isGsa && nf >= 3) {\n    // $G?GSA,mode,fix,sv1..sv12,pdop,hdop,vdop\n    // fields[3..14] are SV IDs used (may be blank)\n    _nmea_used_count = 0;\n    for (int i = 3; i <= 14 && i < nf && _nmea_used_count < 12; i++) {\n      if (fields[i] && strlen(fields[i]) > 0) {\n        _nmea_used_svids[_nmea_used_count++] = (uint8_t)atoi(fields[i]);\n      }\n    }\n    // Update svUsed flag for already-collected satellite entries\n    for (uint8_t i = 0; i < data.nmea_sat_count; i++) {\n      data.nmea_sats[i].svUsed = false;\n      for (uint8_t j = 0; j < _nmea_used_count; j++) {\n        if (_nmea_used_svids[j] == data.nmea_sats[i].svId) {\n          data.nmea_sats[i].svUsed = true;\n          break;\n        }\n      }\n    }\n    return true;\n  }\n\n  return false;\n}"
}

  "new_string": "NavsatCollector::NavsatCollector(TeenyUbloxConnect& gnss, const GnssData& gps_data) : Worker<NavsatData>({\n                                                                .available=false,\n                                                                .navsat_info=ubloxNAVSATInfo_t(),\n                                                            }), _gnss(gnss), _gps_data(gps_data) {\n}\n\n/**\n * @return true if auto NAVSAT has been set, only if GPS module is already initialized.\n*/\nbool NavsatCollector::activate(bool retry) {\n  if (_gps_data.nmea_mode) {\n    return true; // NMEA mode: always activate, data comes from GPS connector NMEA parsing\n  }\n  if (_gnss.pollUART1Port()) {\n    return _gnss.setAutoNAVSATRate(10);\n  }\n  return false;\n}\n\nvoid NavsatCollector::deactivate() {\n  if (_gps_data.nmea_mode) {\n    return;\n  }\n  _gnss.setAutoNAVSAT(false);\n}\n\nint8_t NavsatCollector::produce_data() {\n  if (_gps_data.nmea_mode) {\n    // Build navsat_info from NMEA $GPGSV/$GPGSA data collected by GpsConnector\n    if (_gps_data.nmea_sat_count == 0) return e_worker_idle;\n    auto& info = data.navsat_info;\n    info.validPacket = true;\n    info.numSvs = min(_gps_data.nmea_sat_count, (uint8_t)UBX_MAXNAVSATSATELLITES);\n    info.numSvsReceived = info.numSvs;\n    info.numSvsEphValid = info.numSvs;\n    info.numSvsHealthy = 0;\n    info.numSvsUsed = 0;\n    for (uint8_t i = 0; i < info.numSvs; i++) {\n      const auto& s = _gps_data.nmea_sats[i];\n      info.svSortList[i].svId       = s.svId;\n      info.svSortList[i].gnssIdType = s.gnssIdType;\n      info.svSortList[i].elev       = s.elev;\n      info.svSortList[i].azim       = s.azim;\n      info.svSortList[i].cno        = s.cno;\n      info.svSortList[i].svUsed     = s.svUsed;\n      info.svSortList[i].healthy    = (s.cno > 0);\n      info.svSortList[i].ephValid   = true;\n      if (s.svUsed)  info.numSvsUsed++;\n      if (s.cno > 0) info.numSvsHealthy++;\n    }\n    data.available = true;\n    return e_worker_data_read;\n  }\n\n  // UBX mode: collect NAV-SAT packet from module\n  if (_gnss.getNAVSAT()) {\n    _gnss.getNAVSATInfo(data.navsat_info);\n    data.available = data.navsat_info.validPacket;\n    return e_worker_data_read;\n  }\n  return e_worker_idle;\n}"
}

> AGENT

Pushed. Flash the new build and the satellite constellation map should now show your satellites with their positions, signal strength rings, and which ones are used in the nav solution.

What's being parsed and displayed per satellite from the NMEA sentences:
- **Position on map** — from `$GPGSV` azimuth + elevation
- **Signal ring colour** — white/yellow/orange based on SNR (`cno`) from `$GPGSV`
- **Fill colour** — blue if used in nav fix (from `$GPGSA`), red if tracked but not used
- **Label** — `G` prefix + SV ID

202	void SurveyModeScreen::enter_screen(Controller& controller) {
203	  BaseScreen::enter_screen(controller);
204	  
205	  // Enter low power mode for Survey
206	  // Note: Core2 runs at 80MHz always (set at boot), CoreS3 reduces to 80MHz here

208

210	    // Automatically start logging

212	  }
213	  
214	  // We'll set the GPS to PEDESTRIAN mode in the first render call
215	  // when we have access to the worker map and save it to flash memory
216	  // No message displayed when entering Survey mode
217	  force_next_render(); // Force render to apply GPS settings
218	}

66	  if (!retry) {
67	#ifdef CONFIG_IDF_TARGET_ESP32S3 // Core S3SE fix using the correct serial pins
68	    _serial_conn.begin(38400, SERIAL_8N1, RXD2, TXD2);
69	#else
70	    _serial_conn.begin(38400);
71	#endif
72	
73	    _init_at = millis();
74	  }
75	  if (_tried_115200_at == 0 && (millis() - _init_at > 1000)) { // Wait for device to completely startup
76	    _tried_115200_at = millis();
77	    _serial_conn.updateBaudRate(115200);
78	    M5_LOGD("GNSS: Try at 115200 baud");
79	    if (_gnss.begin(_serial_conn, 500)) {
80	      M5_LOGD("GNSS: connected at 115200 baud"); // no need to set module to 38.4
81	    }
82	    else {
83	      return false;
84	    }
85	  }

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/bGeigieZen/bgeigiezen_firmware/workers/gps_connector.cpp",

}

Builds cleanly. The IDE diagnostics are false positives from the clang analyzer's inability to fully resolve template member `data` in certain positions — the actual GCC compiler has no issue with it.

- In full UBX mode: `GNSS: GPS module: u-blox M7, protocol v14.00` (or M8/M9/M10 depending on module)

32	BaseScreen* SatelliteViewScreen::handle_input(Controller& controller, const worker_map_t& workers) {
33	
34	  if (menu_open()) {
35	    return handle_menu_input(controller, workers, SATELLITE_MENU, e_satellite_MENU_MAX);
36	  }
37	  else {
38	    const auto gps = workers.worker<GpsConnector>(k_worker_gps_connector);
39	    const auto navsat = workers.worker<NavsatCollector>(k_worker_navsat_collector);
40	
41	    if (gps->active() && !navsat->active()) {
42	      // reconnect navsat worker once gps worker is connected
43	      // M5_LOGD("NAVSAT: Activating navsat collector");
44	      controller.set_worker_active(k_worker_navsat_collector, true);
45	      if (navsat->active()) {
46	        set_status_message(F(" Nav-sat connected, this can take a few seconds "));
47	        // M5_LOGD("NAVSAT: Successfully activated");
48	      } else {
49	        set_status_message(F(" Nav-sat was unable to connect "));
50	        // M5_LOGD("NAVSAT: Failed to activate");
51	      }
52	    }
53	
54	    const auto button1 = workers.worker<ZenButton>(k_worker_button_1);
55	    const auto button2 = workers.worker<ZenButton>(k_worker_button_2);
56	    const auto button3 = workers.worker<ZenButton>(k_worker_button_3);
57	    if (button1->is_fresh() && button1->get_data().shortPress) {
58	      open_menu(true);
59	      // Don't clear the entire screen to avoid flickering
60	      // The menu render will handle clearing what it needs
61	      force_next_render();
62	    }
63	    if (button2->is_fresh() && button2->get_data().shortPress) {
64	      // screen specific action
65	      switch (_current_page) {
66	        case e_satellite_page_main:
67	          controller.set_worker_active(k_worker_navsat_collector, false);
68	          controller.set_worker_active(k_worker_gps_connector, false);
69	          controller.set_worker_active(k_worker_gps_connector, true);
70	          controller.set_worker_active(k_worker_navsat_collector, true);
71	          force_next_render();
72	          break;
73	        default:
74	          break;
75	      }
76	    }
77	    if (button3->is_fresh() && button3->get_data().shortPress) {
78	      return &MenuWindow_i;
79	    }
80	  }
81	
82	  return nullptr;
83	}

127	    // draw lines at 0, 45, 90, 135 etc degrees azimuth

130	      yCoord = round(cos(radians((i * 45) + 180 + compAngle)) * mapRadius);

139	        case 2: label = 'E'; break;
140	        case 4: label = 'S'; break;
141	        case 6: label = 'W'; break;
142	      }

151	
152	      // draw the positions of the sats
153	      for(int16_t i = navsat_info.numSvsEphValid -1; i >= 0; --i) {
154	
155	        // Sat position
156	        ++numSats;
157	        satAzimuth = navsat_info.svSortList[i].azim;
158	        satElevation = navsat_info.svSortList[i].elev;
159	        if(satElevation < 0) satElevation = 0;
160	        xCoord = round(-sin(radians(satAzimuth + 180 + compAngle)) *
161	                       map(satElevation, 0, 90, mapSatRadius, 1));
162	        yCoord = round(cos(radians(satAzimuth + 180 + compAngle)) *
163	                       map(satElevation, 0, 90, mapSatRadius, 1));
164	
165	        // Sat ring color based on SNR
166	        if(navsat_info.svSortList[i].cno >= 35) {
167	          satRingColor = COLOR_SAT_SIGNAL_STRONG;
168	        } else if(navsat_info.svSortList[i].cno >=20) {
169	          satRingColor = COLOR_SAT_SIGNAL_MEDIUM;
170	        } else {
171	          satRingColor = COLOR_SAT_SIGNAL_WEAK;
172	        }
173	        M5.Lcd.fillCircle(xCoord + mapCenterX, yCoord + mapCenterY, satRingRadius, satRingColor);
174	
175	        // Sat color based on svUsed
176	        if(navsat_info.svSortList[i].svUsed) {
177	          satColor = COLOR_SAT_USED_NAV;
178	        } else if(navsat_info.svSortList[i].healthy) {
179	          satColor = COLOR_SAT_HEALTHY;
180	        } else {
181	          satColor = COLOR_SAT_UNKNOWN;
182	        }
183	        M5.Lcd.fillCircle(xCoord + mapCenterX, yCoord + mapCenterY, satRadius, satColor);
184	
185	
186	        // Sat label
187	        M5.Lcd.setTextColor(LCD_COLOR_DEFAULT, satColor);
188	        sprintf(_dispStr, "%c%02d",
189	                navsat_info.svSortList[i].gnssIdType,
190	                navsat_info.svSortList[i].svId);
191	        M5.Lcd.drawString(_dispStr, xCoord + mapCenterX - 8, yCoord + mapCenterY + 5, &fonts::Font0);
192	
193	      }
194	    }
195	  }
196	
197	  if (force) {
198	    // Buttons
199	    drawButton1("Options");
200	    drawButton2("Reconnect");
201	    drawButton3("Menu");
202	
203	    // Legend
204	    M5.Lcd.setTextColor(LCD_COLOR_DEFAULT, LCD_COLOR_BACKGROUND);
205	    M5.Lcd.drawCircle(215, 128 - 9, 5, COLOR_SAT_SIGNAL_STRONG);
206	    M5.Lcd.drawString("Strong signal", 225, 128, &fonts::Font2);
207	    M5.Lcd.drawCircle(215, 144 - 9, 5, COLOR_SAT_SIGNAL_MEDIUM);
208	    M5.Lcd.drawString("Medium signal", 225, 144, &fonts::Font2);
209	    M5.Lcd.drawCircle(215, 160 - 9, 5, COLOR_SAT_SIGNAL_WEAK);
210	    M5.Lcd.drawString("Weak signal", 225, 160, &fonts::Font2);
211	    M5.Lcd.fillCircle(215, 176 - 9, 5, COLOR_SAT_USED_NAV);
212	    M5.Lcd.drawString("Used in nav", 225, 176, &fonts::Font2);
213	    M5.Lcd.fillCircle(215, 192 - 9, 5, COLOR_SAT_HEALTHY);
214	    M5.Lcd.drawString("Tracked", 225, 192, &fonts::Font2);
215	  }
216	}

(uint8_t)atoi(fields[base + 3]) : 0;\n      sat.gnssIdType = gnssIdType;\n      // Mark as used if svId is in the GPGSA used list\n      sat.svUsed = false;\n      for (uint8_t j = 0; j < _nmea_used_count; j++) {\n        if (_nmea_used_svids[j] == sat.svId) { sat.svUsed = true; break; }\n      }\n    }\n    return true;\n  }",

> AGENT

Builds clean. All those IDE errors were false positives from the IDE not using the ESP32 toolchain for analysis.

Summary of changes:
- **`gps_connector.h/cpp`**: Added `nmea_gsv_cycle` counter, incremented only when the last message of a complete GPGSV sequence arrives
- **`navsat_collector.h/cpp`**: Tracks `_last_gsv_cycle` and only fires `e_worker_data_read` once per complete satellite cycle (~1/sec) instead of every NMEA sentence (~7/sec)
- **`satellite_view.cpp`**: Renders the constellation map into a static `M5Canvas` sprite and pushes it atomically — the LCD sees one blit instead of clear→draw→clear→draw

Flash and the satellite screen should be flicker-free.

> AGENT

**Fix 1 — center text**: use `middle_center` text datum on the sprite so labels render centered in the satellite circle instead of offset below it.

**Fix 2 — status bar count**: in NMEA mode `satsInView` is set from GPGSV total (12); in UBX mode it comes from NAV-PVT `numSV` which is sats-used-in-fix. Make NMEA consistent by updating `satsInView` from GPGSA's used count.

**Issue 3** — positions DO update each GPGSV cycle; satellites just move very slowly in the sky (~1°/min), so within a minute of observation no movement is visible. Colors (cno signal strength) fluctuate much faster. This is expected behavior.

> AGENT

Clean build. Flash and test:

- Satellite labels should now be centered inside the circles
- Status bar `GPS5` (used in fix) instead of `GPS12` (total visible)
- Satellite positions appear static over short periods — this is correct, they move ~1° per minute in the sky

> DEVELOPER

To be sure, theM8 and M0 co GPS modules will still work as before?

> AGENT

Yes, M8 and M10 in UBX mode are untouched functionally. Here's why each change is safe:

| Change | UBX mode impact |
|---|---|
| `nmea_gsv_cycle` field added to GnssData | Initialized to 0, never modified in UBX mode |
| Cycle counter in GSV/GSA parsing | Inside `parseNmeaSentence()` → only called from `produceDataNmea()` → only called when `_nmea_mode == true` |
| `data.satsInView = _nmea_used_count` | Same — inside `parseNmeaSentence()`, never reached in UBX mode |
| `_last_gsv_cycle` in NavsatCollector | NMEA branch guarded by `if (_gps_data.nmea_mode)` — UBX mode goes straight to `if (_gnss.getNAVSAT())` as before |
| Log message (module identification) | Cosmetic only, runs after `_gnss.begin()` succeeds, no functional change |
| Sprite rendering + centered labels | Display-only improvement, applies to both modes, no functional change |

The UBX code path in `navsat_collector.cpp` is byte-for-byte identical to what it was before these changes.

> DEVELOPER

To be sure, the M8 and M10 co GPS modules will still work as before?

> AGENT

Yes, confirmed — same answer as above. All changes are either:

- Guarded by `if (_gps_data.nmea_mode)` / `if (_nmea_mode)` — UBX code never enters those branches
- Pure display improvements (sprite rendering, label centering) that apply equally to both modes

M8 and M10 UBX mode behavior is unchanged.

> DEVELOPER

The text is nicely centered now. But still the status bar mentione 12 while the satelite view correctly shows 5 active for navigation . I think the 5 for navigation is correct. 12 is atll sateites in view?

> AGENT

The fix didn't stick because the GPGSV parser overwrites it immediately after. The sentence order from u-blox is:

```
$GPGSA → satsInView = 5  ✓
$GPGSV msgNum=1 → satsInView = 12  ✗ (overwrites!)
```

The fix is to stop GPGSV from writing to `satsInView` — use `numSV` for total visible instead:

207	          new_screen = new_screen->handle_input(_controller, workers);
208	        }
209	      }
210	      if (new_screen && new_screen != _screen) {
211	        M5_LOGD("New screen entered: %s", new_screen->get_title());
212	        _screen->leave_screen(_controller);
213	        _screen = new_screen;
214	        clear();
215	        _screen->enter_screen(_controller);
216	
217	        // Check if operational mode and save
218	        if (new_screen == &DriveModeScreen_i) {
219	          _settings.set_last_mode(LocalStorage::e_operational_mode_drive, false);
220	        }
221	        if (new_screen == &SurveyModeScreen_i) {
222	          _settings.set_last_mode(LocalStorage::e_operational_mode_survey, false);
223	        }
224	        if (new_screen == &FixedModeScreen_i) {
225	          _settings.set_last_mode(LocalStorage::e_operational_mode_fixed, false);
226	        }
227	        if (new_screen == &SatelliteViewScreen_i) {
228	          _settings.set_last_mode(LocalStorage::e_operational_mode_satellite, false);
229	        }
230	
231	      }
232	    }
233	
234	    if (workers.any_updates() || handlers.any_updates() || (millis() - _last_render > LCD_REFRESH_RATE)) {
235	      M5.Lcd.startWrite();
236	      M5.Lcd.setRotation(3);
237	      if (_menu->menu_open()) {
238	        _menu->do_render(workers, handlers);
239	      } else {
240	        _screen->do_render(workers, handlers);
241	      }
242	
243	      if (_screen->has_status_bar()) {
244	        // Only update status bar at a reduced rate to prevent flickering
245	        static unsigned long last_status_bar_update = 0;
246	        static bool status_bar_needs_full_redraw = true;
247	        static bool message_displayed = false;
248	        static String last_error_message = "";
249	        static String last_status_message = "";
250	        static unsigned long message_display_time = 0;
251	        static const unsigned long MESSAGE_TIMEOUT = 5000; // 5 seconds timeout for messages
252	        static BaseScreen* last_error_screen = nullptr; // Track which screen showed the error
253	        static String last_shown_error = ""; // Track the last error message that was actually shown
254	        
255	        // Define message area properties based on Font2 (height 16px) and current baseline 220
256	        const int MSG_AREA_Y_BASELINE = 220;
257	        const int MSG_AREA_FONT_HEIGHT = 16; // M5.Lcd.fontHeight(&fonts::Font2) is 16
258	        const int MSG_AREA_Y_TOP = MSG_AREA_Y_BASELINE - MSG_AREA_FONT_HEIGHT;
259	        const int MSG_AREA_HEIGHT = MSG_AREA_FONT_HEIGHT;
260	        
261	        // Get current messages from the screen
262	        const __FlashStringHelper* error_msg = _screen->get_error_message(workers, handlers);
263	        const __FlashStringHelper* status_msg = _screen->get_status_message(workers, handlers);
264	        
265	        String current_error_message = error_msg ? String(error_msg) : "";
266	        String current_status_message = status_msg ? String(status_msg) : "";
267	        
268	        // Check if messages have changed
269	        bool message_changed = (current_error_message != last_error_message) || (current_status_message != last_status_message);

21	/**
22	 * Base class for the worker
23	 * To use this, extend the Worker class
24	 */
25	class BaseWorker : public Activatable {
26	 public:
27	
28	  /**
29	   * Enum can be replaced with custom implementation
30	   * async running and idle states are used by the worker itself (at -2 and -1 respectively)
31	   * Data read = 0, considered fresh produced data to be handled
32	   * Anything above 0 can be used for custom errors
33	   */
34	  typedef enum Status {
35	    e_worker_processing = -2, // For async
36	    e_worker_idle = -1,
37	    e_worker_data_read = 0,
38	    e_worker_error,
39	  } Status;
40	
41	  explicit BaseWorker(uint32_t break_duration = 0);
42	  virtual ~BaseWorker() = default;
43	
44	  explicit BaseWorker(BaseWorker& copy) = delete;
45	
46	
47	  /**
48	   * Get status
49	   * @return
50	   */
51	  int8_t get_status() const;
52	
53	  /**
54	   * Get status
55	   * @return
56	   */
57	  uint32_t get_last_produce() const;
58	
59	  /**
60	   * Checks if fresh data is read
61	   * @return
62	   */
63	  bool is_fresh() const;
64	
65	 protected:
66	  /**
67	   * The main function to implement in sub classes, store produced work in `data` property
68	   * Used by normal workers
69	   * @return status code (BaseWorker::Status or any custom)
70	   */
71	  virtual int8_t produce_data() = 0;
72	
73	  /**
74	   * The main function to implement in sub classes, store produced work in `data` property
75	   * Used by process workers
76	   * @return status code (BaseWorker::Status or any custom)
77	   */
78	  virtual int8_t produce_data(const worker_map_t& workers) = 0;
79	
80	  /**
81	   * Function that is run async in a separate task, do NOT access worker.data from this
82	   * prepare data inside the instance, to be copied afterwards in the main loop by implementing `finish_produced_data`
83	   * @return status code (BaseWorker::Status or any custom)
84	   */
85	  virtual int8_t produce_async_data();
86	
87	  /**
88	   * Implement this function to finalize async results to the data to be used in the rest of the system.
89	   * This function is called by the main thread after the task to produce async data was finished.
90	   */
91	  virtual void finish_produced_data();
92	
93	  virtual bool is_process_worker() const = 0;
94	
95	  int8_t start_task(const char* task_name, uint32_t memory=1024, uint8_t priority=5, uint8_t core=0);
96	  void kill_task();
97	
98	  virtual bool task_running() const;
99	
100	 private:
101	
102	  /**
103	   * Called by the aggregator to get new data. Will fill in the work report depending on its state and produced work
104	   * @return true if new data was produced.
105	   */
106	  bool work(const worker_map_t& workers);
107	
108	  uint32_t break_duration;
109	  uint32_t last_produce;
110	  int8_t status;
111	  int8_t async_result_status;
112	
113	 private:
114	
115	  static void run_task(void* instance);
116	
117	  TaskHandle_t xAsyncWorkerHandle;
118	
119	  friend Aggregator;
120	};
121	
122	/**
123	 * A worker will do things to get fresh data in the system. This can be retrieving sensor data, keeping track of
124	 * interrupts or reading serial data.
125	 * @tparam T: Type of the data it produces (can be int, string, or some struct type)
126	 */
127	template<typename T>
128	class Worker : public BaseWorker {
129	 public:
130	  /**
131	   *
132	   * Construct a worker, calls default constructor for data
133	   * @param worker_id : unique id of the worker
134	   * @param break_duration : time in millis how long the delay should be between produced work, default 0.
135	   */
136	  explicit Worker(uint32_t break_duration = 0)
137	      : BaseWorker(break_duration), data() {
138	  }
139	
140	  /**
141	   * Construct a worker with initial data T
142	   * @param worker_id : unique id of the worker
143	   * @param initial_val : initial value of the data it produces
144	   * @param break_duration : time in millis how long the delay should be between produced work, default 0.
145	   */
146	  explicit Worker(T initial_val, uint32_t break_duration = 0)
147	      : BaseWorker(break_duration), data(initial_val) {
148	  }
149	
150	  virtual ~Worker() {
151	  };
152	
153	  /**
154	   * Get the current data from the worker
155	   * @return current data
156	   */
157	  const T& get_data() {
158	    return data;
159	  }
160	
161	 protected:
162	  bool is_process_worker() const override {
163	    return false;
164	  }
165	
166	  T data;
167	
168	 private:
169	  virtual int8_t produce_data(const worker_map_t& workers) {
170	    // Process worker will be called with produce_data(workers)
171	    return e_worker_idle;
172	  };
173	
174	};
175	
176	/**
177	 * A worker will do things to get fresh data in the system. This can be retrieving sensor data, keeping track of
178	 * interrupts or reading serial data.
179	 * @tparam T: Type of the data it produces (can be int, string, or some struct type)
180	 */
181	template<typename T>
182	class ProcessWorker : public Worker<T> {
183	 public:
184	  /**
185	   *
186	   * Construct a worker, calls default constructor for data
187	   * @param worker_id : unique id of the worker
188	   * @param break_duration : time in millis how long the delay should be between produced work, default 0.
189	   */
190	  explicit ProcessWorker(uint32_t break_duration = 0)
191	      : Worker<T>(break_duration) {
192	  }
193	
194	  /**
195	   * Construct a worker with initial data T
196	   * @param worker_id : unique id of the worker
197	   * @param initial_val : initial value of the data it produces
198	   * @param break_duration : time in millis how long the delay should be between produced work, default 0.
199	   */
200	  explicit ProcessWorker(T initial_val, uint32_t break_duration = 0)
201	      : Worker<T>(initial_val, break_duration) {
202	  }
203	
204	  virtual ~ProcessWorker() = default;
205	
206	 protected:
207	  bool is_process_worker() const override {
208	    return true;
209	  }
210	
211	  virtual int8_t produce_data(const WorkerMap& workers) = 0;
212	
213	 private:
214	  virtual int8_t produce_data() final {
215	    // Process worker will be called with produce_data(workers)
216	    return BaseWorker::e_worker_idle;
217	  };
218	
219	  friend Aggregator;
220	};
221	
222	class WorkerMap : public std::map<uint8_t, BaseWorker*> {
223	 public:
224	
225	  template<typename T, typename std::enable_if<std::is_base_of<BaseWorker, T>::value>::type* = nullptr>
226	  T* worker(uint8_t idx) const {
227	    return (T*) at(idx);
228	  }
229	
230	  bool any_updates() const;
231	};
232	
233	#endif //SENSOR_REPORTER_SENSOR_HPP_

1	//
2	// Created by Jelle Bouwhuis on 12/24/19.
3	//
4	
5	#include "Worker.hpp"
6	
7	BaseWorker::BaseWorker(uint32_t break_duration)
8	    : Activatable(), break_duration(break_duration), last_produce(0), status(Status::e_worker_idle), async_result_status(Status::e_worker_idle) {
9	}
10	
11	int8_t BaseWorker::get_status() const {
12	  return status;
13	}
14	
15	uint32_t BaseWorker::get_last_produce() const {
16	  return last_produce;
17	}
18	
19	bool BaseWorker::is_fresh() const {
20	  return get_active_state() == e_state_active && status == e_worker_data_read;
21	}
22	
23	int8_t BaseWorker::produce_async_data() {
24	  return e_worker_idle;
25	}
26	
27	bool BaseWorker::work(const worker_map_t& workers) {
28	  if (get_active_state() == e_state_activating_failed) {
29	    // Still activating, will try to activate again
30	    set_active(true);
31	  }
32	  if (active()) {
33	    if (task_running()) {
34	      // Task running async, skip working
35	      return false;
36	    } else if (status == e_worker_processing) {
37	      // Task completed async, prepare data to be used in system
38	      status = async_result_status;
39	      async_result_status = e_worker_idle;
40	      finish_produced_data();
41	    } else {
42	      // Normal work process
43	      if ((millis() - last_produce > break_duration || last_produce == 0)) {
44	        status = is_process_worker() ? produce_data(workers) : produce_data();
45	      }
46	      else {
47	        status = e_worker_idle;
48	      }
49	    }
50	    if (is_fresh()) {
51	      // Work has been produced
52	      last_produce = millis();
53	      return true;
54	    }
55	  }
56	  return false;
57	}
58	
59	int8_t BaseWorker::start_task(const char* task_name, uint32_t memory, uint8_t priority, uint8_t core) {
60	  if (xAsyncWorkerHandle != nullptr) {
61	    return e_worker_processing; // Already working
62	  }
63	  xTaskCreatePinnedToCore(BaseWorker::run_task, task_name, memory, this, priority, &xAsyncWorkerHandle, core);
64	  return e_worker_processing;
65	}
66	
67	void BaseWorker::kill_task() {
68	  if (xAsyncWorkerHandle != nullptr) {
69	    vTaskDelete(xAsyncWorkerHandle);
70	    xAsyncWorkerHandle = nullptr;
71	  }
72	}
73	
74	bool BaseWorker::task_running() const {
75	  return xAsyncWorkerHandle != nullptr;
76	}
77	
78	void BaseWorker::run_task(void* instance) {
79	  auto worker = (BaseWorker*) instance;
80	  worker->async_result_status = worker->produce_async_data();
81	  worker->xAsyncWorkerHandle = nullptr;
82	  vTaskDelete(nullptr);
83	}
84	
85	void BaseWorker::finish_produced_data() {
86	}
87	
88	bool WorkerMap::any_updates() const {
89	  return std::any_of(
90	      begin(),
91	      end(),
92	      [](const std::pair<uint8_t, BaseWorker*>& pair) {
93	        return pair.second->get_status() != BaseWorker::e_worker_idle;
94	      }
95	  );
96	}

> AGENT

Found the real issue. Before my last fix, GPGSV set `satsInView` to total visible (12). Now only GPGSA sets it, which is 0 before a fix (no satellites used yet). So the user correctly sees GPS0 before fix, GPS5 after — but the GPS0 looks broken.

The right behavior:
- **No fix** (orange): show total visible (`numSV` from GPGSV, e.g. GPS8 — "searching")
- **Fix acquired** (green): show used-in-fix (`satsInView` from GPGSA/GGA, e.g. GPS5)

The `numSV` field is already correctly set: GPGSV writes it last in each cycle, so it always holds the total visible count. Just need to choose which field to display based on `location_valid`:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01LasY1M4KS9XR6uXtJzGAm6

  "new_string": "          } else {\n            bool has_fix = gps->get_data().location_valid;\n            M5.Lcd.setTextColor(has_fix ? LCD_COLOR_ACTIVITY : LCD_COLOR_STALE_INCOMPLETE, TFT_BLACK);\n            // Before fix: show total visible (numSV) so the user sees the GPS is searching.\n            // After fix: show used-in-fix (satsInView) which is the meaningful count.\n            uint8_t display_sats = has_fix ? gps->get_data().satsInView : gps->get_data().numSV;\n            M5.Lcd.printf(\"GPS%d\", display_sats);\n            pos += 34;\n          }"
}
