31	void PowerManager::enterLowPowerMode(bool wifi_required) {
32	    if (_low_power_mode) {
33	        M5_LOGW("Already in low power mode");
34	        return;
35	    }
36	
37	    M5_LOGI("Entering low power mode (WiFi %s)", wifi_required ? "required" : "not required");
38	    logPowerState(); // Log initial state
39	
40	    // 1. Set CPU frequency for power savings
41	    #ifdef CONFIG_IDF_TARGET_ESP32S3
42	        // ESP32-S3 supports 80, 160, 240 MHz natively. We'll use 80MHz for stable low power.
43	        setCpuFrequency(80);
44	        logPowerState(); // Log after CPU frequency change
45	    #else
46	        // For Core2 (ESP32):
47	        // CPU is set to 80MHz at boot and NEVER changed
48	        // WiFi buffer allocation happens at 80MHz and stays stable
49	        // This provides ~33% power savings compared to 240MHz
50	        M5_LOGI("Core2: Keeping CPU at 80MHz (set at boot) for power savings and WiFi stability");
51	    #endif
52	
53	    // 2. Reduce I2C clock speed (logical only; transfers specify freq per call)
54	    setI2cClock(50000);  // 50kHz logical target
55	    logPowerState(); // Log after I2C clock change
56	
57	    // 3. Wireless modules will be managed by specific modes
58	    
59	    // 4. Reduce logging to minimum, but keep WiFi debug logging
60	    esp_log_level_set("*", ESP_LOG_ERROR);
61	    esp_log_level_set("wifi", ESP_LOG_DEBUG); // Set WiFi logging to DEBUG
62	    
63	    _low_power_mode = true;
64	    M5_LOGI("Low power mode activated");
65	    logPowerState(); // Log final state
66	}
67	
68	void PowerManager::exitLowPowerMode() {
69	    if (!_low_power_mode) {
70	        M5_LOGW("Not in low power mode");
71	        return;
72	    }
73	
74	    M5_LOGI("Exiting low power mode");
75	
76	    // 1. Restore CPU frequency
77	    #ifdef CONFIG_IDF_TARGET_ESP32S3
78	    // CoreS3 can restore to any frequency
79	    if (_original_cpu_freq > 0) {
80	        setCpuFrequency(_original_cpu_freq);
81	    }
82	    #else
83	    // Core2: Don't change CPU frequency (always 80MHz for WiFi stability)
84	    M5_LOGI("Core2: Keeping CPU at 80MHz for WiFi stability");
85	    #endif
86	
87	    // 2. Restore I2C clock speed (logical only)
88	    if (_original_i2c_freq > 0) {
89	        setI2cClock(_original_i2c_freq);
90	    }
91	
92	    // Restore normal logging
93	    esp_log_level_set("*", ESP_LOG_INFO);
94	    esp_log_level_set("wifi", ESP_LOG_INFO); // Restore WiFi logging to INFO
95	
96	    // Note: WiFi will be initialized on-demand when needed by config mode or other features
97	    // We don't force initialization here to avoid memory allocation issues
98	
99	    _low_power_mode = false;
100	    M5_LOGI("Normal power mode restored");

106	    }
107	    
108	    // Store the original frequency if not already stored
109	    if (_original_cpu_freq == 0) {
110	        _original_cpu_freq = getCpuFrequencyMhz();
111	    }
112	    
113	    // Check if frequency is already set
114	    if (getCpuFrequencyMhz() == freq_mhz) {
115	        M5_LOGI("CPU frequency already set to %u MHz", freq_mhz);
116	        return true;
117	    }
118	    
119	    // Validate frequency for ESP32-S3
120	    bool valid_freq = false;
121	    #ifdef CONFIG_IDF_TARGET_ESP32S3
122	        // ESP32-S3 supported frequencies
123	        if (freq_mhz == 80 || freq_mhz == 160 || freq_mhz == 240 || freq_mhz == 40) {
124	            valid_freq = true;
125	        }
126	    #else
127	        // Original ESP32 frequencies (Core2)
128	        if (freq_mhz == 80 || freq_mhz == 160 || freq_mhz == 240 || freq_mhz == 40) {
129	            valid_freq = true;
130	        }
131	    #endif
132	    
133	    if (!valid_freq) {
134	        #ifdef CONFIG_IDF_TARGET_ESP32S3
135	            M5_LOGW("Unsupported CPU frequency: %u MHz for S3, using 80MHz", freq_mhz);
136	            freq_mhz = 80;
137	        #else
138	            // For Core2 (ESP32), default to 160MHz if unsupported frequency is requested.
139	            M5_LOGW("Unsupported CPU frequency: %u MHz for ESP32 (Core2), using 160MHz", freq_mhz);
140	            freq_mhz = 160;
141	        #endif
142	    }
143	    
144	    M5_LOGI("Attempting to change CPU frequency from %u MHz to %u MHz", 
145	            getCpuFrequencyMhz(), freq_mhz);
146	    
147	    bool success = setCpuFrequencyMhz(freq_mhz); // Re-enable actual CPU frequency change
148	    M5_LOGI("CPU frequency change attempted. Result: %s", success ? "SUCCESS" : "FAILED");
149	
150	    // Verify
151	    if (getCpuFrequencyMhz() != freq_mhz) {
152	        M5_LOGE("Failed to verify CPU frequency set to %u MHz", freq_mhz);
153	        return false;
154	    }
155	    
156	    return true;
157	}

159	uint32_t PowerManager::getCpuFrequency() {
160	    // Use the appropriate method based on the ESP32 variant
161	    #ifdef CONFIG_IDF_TARGET_ESP32S3
162	        return getCpuFrequencyMhz();
163	    #else
164	        return getCpuFrequencyMhz();

168	void PowerManager::disableWireless() {
169	    ESP_LOGI("PowerMgr", "Disabling wireless modules");
170	
171	    // Disable WiFi
172	    #ifdef CONFIG_IDF_TARGET_ESP32S3
173	        // CoreS3 can safely deinit and reinit WiFi
174	        WiFi.mode(WIFI_OFF);
175	        esp_wifi_stop();
176	        esp_wifi_deinit();
177	    #else
178	        // Core2 (ESP32): NEVER call WiFi.mode() or esp_wifi_deinit()
179	        // Both trigger deinitialization which corrupts RX buffers
180	        // Just disconnect and stop the radio - driver stays initialized
181	        WiFi.disconnect(true);  // Disconnect from AP
182	        esp_wifi_stop();        // Stop WiFi radio (but don't deinit)
183	        ESP_LOGI("PowerMgr", "Core2: WiFi stopped but driver kept initialized for stability");
184	    #endif
185	
186	    // Disable Bluetooth if it was enabled
187	    if (btStarted()) {
188	        btStop();
189	    }
190	}
191	
192	void PowerManager::enableWireless() {
193	    ESP_LOGI("PowerMgr", "Wireless modules will be re-enabled when needed by specific modes");
194	    // Note: WiFi and BT are enabled on-demand by the specific features that need them
195	}

197	void PowerManager::setI2cClock(uint32_t freq_hz) {
198	    if (freq_hz < 10000 || freq_hz > 1000000) {
199	        ESP_LOGW("PowerMgr", "I2C frequency %u Hz is outside recommended range (10kHz - 1MHz)", freq_hz);
200	    }
201	    // Maintain logical frequency; M5Unified calls pass desired freq per transaction.
202	    if (!_low_power_mode) {
203	        _original_i2c_freq = freq_hz;
204	    }
205	    ESP_LOGI("PowerMgr", "Logical I2C clock set to %u Hz", freq_hz);
206	    _last_i2c_freq = freq_hz;
207	}

222	}

225	    // Return the last logical value; avoid querying uninitialized bus.

235	void PowerManager::restoreNormalSettings() {
236	    // Restore CPU frequency
237	    if (_original_cpu_freq > 0) {
238	        setCpuFrequency(_original_cpu_freq);
239	    }

241	    // Restore I2C clock speed

> AGENT

The real issue: our protocol version patch accepts `protocolVersionHigh = 0` (which means "failed to get version from GPS") as a valid M7, so `_gnss.begin()` succeeds at 9600 on any device — including the M7 in NMEA-only mode. The device then thinks it's in UBX mode, switches to 38400 baud (command ignored by GPS since TX is unconnected), and `produce_data()` never gets UBX data → GPS0 forever.

Let me verify this in the TeenyUbloxConnect patch:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_0124Digc2Z6L4PDYbMf4zGx9
```json
{
  "command": "grep -n \"protocolVersionHigh\\|M8_MODULE\\|M7\\|< 18\\|return true\\|return false\" /home/rob/Documents/Safecast/bGeigieZen/.pio/libdeps/m5stack-cores3-unified/TeenyUbloxConnect/src/TeenyUbloxConnect.cpp | head -30",
  "description": "Check protocol version patch in TeenyUbloxConnect"
}
```

> AGENT

Confirmed. Line 56: `else if(protocolVersionHigh < 18)` matches version `0` (when `getModuleInfo()` fails — GPS ignores UBX poll because TX is unconnected). This causes `begin()` to falsely return `true`, putting the device into UBX mode at 38400 baud while the GPS stays at 9600 NMEA → GPS0 forever.

Fix both library copies:

> TOOL

tool_use Read
id: toolu_01UTApMMo8uYYSwLnfTV5sfK
```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/bGeigieZen/.pio/libdeps/m5stack-cores3-unified/TeenyUbloxConnect/src/TeenyUbloxConnect.cpp",
  "offset": 44,
  "limit": 25
}
```

> TOOL

49	        return true;
50	      } else if((protocolVersionHigh == 32) && (protocolVersionLow == 01)) {

> AGENT

Clean build. 

**Root cause found**: `protocolVersionHigh == 0` means the GPS didn't respond to the UBX poll (because its RX line is unconnected). Our `< 18` patch matched `0`, causing `begin()` to falsely succeed, putting the device in UBX mode at 38400 baud while the GPS stayed at 9600 NMEA. UBX mode never got data → GPS0 forever.

**Fix**: added `> 0 &&` so version 0 (no response) falls through to `return false`, allowing the baud-rate loop to complete and the TX test to detect NMEA-only mode correctly.

After flashing, the GPS0 on boot should resolve on its own within a few seconds — no mode switch needed.

31	bool TeenyUbloxConnect::begin(Stream &serialPort_, uint16_t maxWait_) {
32	  serialPort = &serialPort_;
33	  resetNAVSTATUSInfo(); // reset spoofing flags
34	  ubloxModuleType = UBLOX_UNKNOWN_MODULE;
35	  ubloxCFGGNSSInfoValid = false;
36	  // Try three times
37	  bool connected = false;

39	    connected = true;
40	  } else if(pollUART1Port_M8(maxWait_, true)) {
41	    connected = true;
42	  } else if(pollUART1Port_M8(maxWait_, true)) {
43	    connected = true;
44	  }
45	  if(connected) {
46	    if(pollProtocolVersion(maxWait_)) {
47	      if(protocolVersionHigh == 18) {
48	        ubloxModuleType = UBLOX_M8_MODULE;
49	        return true;
50	      } else if((protocolVersionHigh == 32) && (protocolVersionLow == 01)) {
51	        ubloxModuleType = UBLOX_M9_MODULE;
52	        return true;

54	        ubloxModuleType = UBLOX_M10_MODULE;
55	        return true;

60	        // so we must NOT accept version 0 here — that falls through to return false.
61	        ubloxModuleType = UBLOX_M8_MODULE;
62	        return true;
63	      }
64	    }
65	  }
66	  return false;

78	/********************************************************************/
79	bool TeenyUbloxConnect::pollUART1Port(uint16_t maxWait_) {
80	  if(ubloxModuleType == UBLOX_M8_MODULE) {
81	    return pollUART1Port_M8(maxWait_);
82	  } else if(ubloxModuleType == UBLOX_M9_MODULE) {
83	    return pollUART1Port_M10(maxWait_);
84	  } else if(ubloxModuleType == UBLOX_M10_MODULE) {
85	    return pollUART1Port_M10(maxWait_);
86	  }
87	  return false;
88	}
89	/********************************************************************/
90	bool TeenyUbloxConnect::pollUART1Port_M8(uint16_t maxWait_, bool flushPort_) {
91	  commandPacket.messageClass = UBX_CLASS_CFG;
92	  commandPacket.messageID = UBX_CFG_PRT;
93	  commandPacket.payloadLength = 1;
94	  commandPacket.payload[0] = COM_PORT_UART1;
95	  commandPacket.validPacket = true;
96	  if(flushPort_) while(serialPort->available()) serialPort->read();
97	  // Use response-only (no ACK required): u-blox 7 does not ACK poll requests
98	  return sendCommandPacket(true, false, maxWait_);
99	}
100	/********************************************************************/
101	bool TeenyUbloxConnect::pollUART1Port_M10(uint16_t maxWait_, bool flushPort_) {
102	  commandPacket.messageClass = UBX_CLASS_CFG;
103	  commandPacket.messageID = UBX_CFG_VALGET;
104	  commandPacket.payloadLength = 8;
105	  commandPacket.payload[0] = 0;
106	  commandPacket.payload[1] = VALGET_LAYER_RAM;
107	  commandPacket.payload[2] = 0;
108	  commandPacket.payload[3] = 0;
109	  for(uint8_t i = 0; i < 4; i++)
110	    commandPacket.payload[4 + i] = UBLOX_CFG_UART1_ENABLED >> (8 * i);
111	  commandPacket.validPacket = true;
112	  if(flushPort_) while(serialPort->available()) serialPort->read();
113	  return sendCommandPacket(true, true, maxWait_);
114	}
115

117	bool TeenyUbloxConnect::setPortOutput(uint8_t portID_, uint8_t comSettings_, uint16_t maxWait_) {
118	  if(ubloxModuleType == UBLOX_M8_MODULE) {
119	    return setPortOutput_M8(portID_, comSettings_, maxWait_);
120	  } else if(ubloxModuleType == UBLOX_M9_MODULE) {
121	    return setPortOutput_M10(portID_, comSettings_, maxWait_);
122	  } else if(ubloxModuleType == UBLOX_M10_MODULE) {
123	    return setPortOutput_M10(portID_, comSettings_, maxWait_);
124	  }

126	}

1514	bool TeenyUbloxConnect::sendCommandPacket(bool expectResp_, bool expectAck_, uint16_t maxWait_) {
1515	  if(!commandPacket.validPacket) return false;
1516	  calcCommandPacketChecksum();
1517	  serialPort->write(commandPacket.synch1);
1518	  serialPort->write(commandPacket.synch2);
1519	  serialPort->write(commandPacket.messageClass);
1520	  serialPort->write(commandPacket.messageID);
1521	  serialPort->write(commandPacket.payloadLength & 0xFF);
1522	  serialPort->write(commandPacket.payloadLength >> 8);
1523	  //for(uint16_t i = 0; i < commandPacket.payloadLength; i++) {
1524	  //  serialPort->write(commandPacket.payload[i]);
1525	  //}
1526	  serialPort->write(commandPacket.payload, commandPacket.payloadLength);
1527	  serialPort->write(commandPacket.checksumA);
1528	  serialPort->write(commandPacket.checksumB);
1529	  commandPacket.validPacket = false;
1530	  if(!(expectResp_ || expectAck_)) {
1531	    return true;
1532	  }
1533	  processingUbloxCommand = true;
1534	  responsePacket.validPacket = false;
1535	  acknowledgePacket.validPacket = false;
1536	  uint32_t startTime = millis();
1537	  while((millis() - startTime) < maxWait_) {
1538	    checkUbloxInternal();
1539	    processIncomingPacket(commandPacket.messageClass, commandPacket.messageID);
1540	    if(expectResp_ && expectAck_ && responsePacket.validPacket && acknowledgePacket.validPacket) {
1541	      processingUbloxCommand = false;
1542	      return acknowledgePacket.messageID;
1543	    } else if(expectResp_ && (!expectAck_) && responsePacket.validPacket) {
1544	      processingUbloxCommand = false;
1545	      return true;
1546	    } else if((!expectResp_) && expectAck_ && acknowledgePacket.validPacket) {
1547	      processingUbloxCommand = false;
1548	      return acknowledgePacket.messageID;
1549	    }
1550	  }
1551	  processingUbloxCommand = false;
1552	  return false;
1553	}

202	void SurveyModeScreen::enter_screen(Controller& controller) {
203	  BaseScreen::enter_screen(controller);
204	  
205	  // Enter low power mode for Survey
206	  // Note: Core2 runs at 80MHz always (set at boot), CoreS3 reduces to 80MHz here
207	  PowerManager::enterLowPowerMode(false);
208	  
209	  if (!controller.get_settings().get_manual_logging()) {
210	    // Automatically start logging
211	    controller.set_handler_active(k_handler_survey_logger, true);
212	  }
213	  
214	  // We'll set the GPS to PEDESTRIAN mode in the first render call
215	  // when we have access to the worker map and save it to flash memory
216	  // No message displayed when entering Survey mode
217	  force_next_render(); // Force render to apply GPS settings
218	}
219	
220	void SurveyModeScreen::leave_screen(Controller& controller) {
221	  // Don't call BaseScreen::leave_screen to avoid displaying a message
222	  
223	  // close logging to file without displaying a message
224	  controller.set_handler_active(k_handler_survey_logger, false);
225	  
226	  // Restore normal power settings
227	  PowerManager::exitLowPowerMode();
228	  
229	  // We can't access the GPS connector directly from here
230	  // The GPS model will be restored in the handle_input method
231	  // when returning to the menu
232	}

502	void GFXScreen::render_screensaver() {
503	  if (_settings.get_animated_screensaver() && millis() - _last_render > 75) {
504	    M5.Lcd.setRotation(3);
505	    if (_saver_x + _saver_x_direction < -2 || _saver_x + _saver_x_direction > 318 - SCREENSAVER_TEXT_LENGTH) {
506	      _saver_x_direction *= -1;
507	    }
508	    if (_saver_y + _saver_y_direction < -2 || _saver_y + _saver_y_direction > 230) {
509	      _saver_y_direction *= -1;
510	    }
511	    _saver_x += _saver_x_direction;
512	    _saver_y += _saver_y_direction;
513	    _saver.pushSprite(_saver_x, _saver_y, TFT_TRANSPARENT);
514	    M5.Lcd.setRotation(1);
515	    _last_render = millis();
516	  }
517	}

21	/**
22	 * Base class for the worker
23	 * To use this, extend the Worker class
24	 */
25	class BaseWorker : public Activatable {
26	 public:
27	
28	  /**
29	   * Enum can be replaced with custom implementation
30	   * async running and idle states are used by the worker itself (at -2 and -1 respectively)
31	   * Data read = 0, considered fresh produced data to be handled
32	   * Anything above 0 can be used for custom errors
33	   */
34	  typedef enum Status {
35	    e_worker_processing = -2, // For async
36	    e_worker_idle = -1,
37	    e_worker_data_read = 0,
38	    e_worker_error,
39	  } Status;
40	
41	  explicit BaseWorker(uint32_t break_duration = 0);
42	  virtual ~BaseWorker() = default;
43	
44	  explicit BaseWorker(BaseWorker& copy) = delete;
45	
46	
47	  /**
48	   * Get status
49	   * @return
50	   */
51	  int8_t get_status() const;
52	
53	  /**
54	   * Get status
55	   * @return
56	   */
57	  uint32_t get_last_produce() const;
58	
59	  /**
60	   * Checks if fresh data is read
61	   * @return
62	   */
63	  bool is_fresh() const;
64	
65	 protected:
66	  /**
67	   * The main function to implement in sub classes, store produced work in `data` property
68	   * Used by normal workers
69	   * @return status code (BaseWorker::Status or any custom)
70	   */
71	  virtual int8_t produce_data() = 0;
72	
73	  /**
74	   * The main function to implement in sub classes, store produced work in `data` property
75	   * Used by process workers
76	   * @return status code (BaseWorker::Status or any custom)
77	   */
78	  virtual int8_t produce_data(const worker_map_t& workers) = 0;
79	
80	  /**
81	   * Function that is run async in a separate task, do NOT access worker.data from this
82	   * prepare data inside the instance, to be copied afterwards in the main loop by implementing `finish_produced_data`
83	   * @return status code (BaseWorker::Status or any custom)
84	   */
85	  virtual int8_t produce_async_data();
86	
87	  /**
88	   * Implement this function to finalize async results to the data to be used in the rest of the system.
89	   * This function is called by the main thread after the task to produce async data was finished.
90	   */
91	  virtual void finish_produced_data();
92	
93	  virtual bool is_process_worker() const = 0;
94	
95	  int8_t start_task(const char* task_name, uint32_t memory=1024, uint8_t priority=5, uint8_t core=0);
96	  void kill_task();
97	
98	  virtual bool task_running() const;
99	
100	 private:
101	
102	  /**
103	   * Called by the aggregator to get new data. Will fill in the work report depending on its state and produced work
104	   * @return true if new data was produced.
105	   */
106	  bool work(const worker_map_t& workers);
107	
108	  uint32_t break_duration;
109	  uint32_t last_produce;
110	  int8_t status;
111	  int8_t async_result_status;
112	
113	 private:
114	
115	  static void run_task(void* instance);
116	
117	  TaskHandle_t xAsyncWorkerHandle;
118	
119	  friend Aggregator;
120	};
121	
122	/**
123	 * A worker will do things to get fresh data in the system. This can be retrieving sensor data, keeping track of
124	 * interrupts or reading serial data.
125	 * @tparam T: Type of the data it produces (can be int, string, or some struct type)
126	 */
127	template<typename T>
128	class Worker : public BaseWorker {
129	 public:
130	  /**
131	   *
132	   * Construct a worker, calls default constructor for data
133	   * @param worker_id : unique id of the worker
134	   * @param break_duration : time in millis how long the delay should be between produced work, default 0.
135	   */
136	  explicit Worker(uint32_t break_duration = 0)
137	      : BaseWorker(break_duration), data() {
138	  }
139	
140	  /**
141	   * Construct a worker with initial data T
142	   * @param worker_id : unique id of the worker
143	   * @param initial_val : initial value of the data it produces
144	   * @param break_duration : time in millis how long the delay should be between produced work, default 0.
145	   */
146	  explicit Worker(T initial_val, uint32_t break_duration = 0)
147	      : BaseWorker(break_duration), data(initial_val) {
148	  }
149	
150	  virtual ~Worker() {
151	  };
152	
153	  /**
154	   * Get the current data from the worker
155	   * @return current data
156	   */
157	  const T& get_data() {
158	    return data;
159	  }
160	
161	 protected:
162	  bool is_process_worker() const override {
163	    return false;
164	  }
165	
166	  T data;
167	
168	 private:
169	  virtual int8_t produce_data(const worker_map_t& workers) {
170	    // Process worker will be called with produce_data(workers)
171	    return e_worker_idle;
172	  };
173	
174	};
175	
176	/**
177	 * A worker will do things to get fresh data in the system. This can be retrieving sensor data, keeping track of
178	 * interrupts or reading serial data.
179	 * @tparam T: Type of the data it produces (can be int, string, or some struct type)
180	 */
181	template<typename T>
182	class ProcessWorker : public Worker<T> {
183	 public:
184	  /**
185	   *
186	   * Construct a worker, calls default constructor for data
187	   * @param worker_id : unique id of the worker
188	   * @param break_duration : time in millis how long the delay should be between produced work, default 0.
189	   */
190	  explicit ProcessWorker(uint32_t break_duration = 0)
191	      : Worker<T>(break_duration) {
192	  }
193	
194	  /**
195	   * Construct a worker with initial data T
196	   * @param worker_id : unique id of the worker
197	   * @param initial_val : initial value of the data it produces
198	   * @param break_duration : time in millis how long the delay should be between produced work, default 0.
199	   */
200	  explicit ProcessWorker(T initial_val, uint32_t break_duration = 0)
201	      : Worker<T>(initial_val, break_duration) {
202	  }
203	
204	  virtual ~ProcessWorker() = default;
205	
206	 protected:
207	  bool is_process_worker() const override {
208	    return true;
209	  }
210	
211	  virtual int8_t produce_data(const WorkerMap& workers) = 0;
212	
213	 private:
214	  virtual int8_t produce_data() final {
215	    // Process worker will be called with produce_data(workers)
216	    return BaseWorker::e_worker_idle;
217	  };
218	
219	  friend Aggregator;
220	};
221	
222	class WorkerMap : public std::map<uint8_t, BaseWorker*> {
223	 public:
224	
225	  template<typename T, typename std::enable_if<std::is_base_of<BaseWorker, T>::value>::type* = nullptr>
226	  T* worker(uint8_t idx) const {
227	    return (T*) at(idx);
228	  }
229	
230	  bool any_updates() const;
231	};
232	
233	#endif //SENSOR_REPORTER_SENSOR_HPP_

1137	int8_t GpsConnector::produceDataNmea() {
1138	  auto ret_status = e_worker_idle;
1139	  static bool first_fix_logged = false;
1140	
1141	  while (_serial_conn.available()) {
1142	    char c = (char)_serial_conn.read();
1143	    if (c == '$') {
1144	      _nmea_len = 0;
1145	      _nmea_buf[_nmea_len++] = c;
1146	    } else if (c == '\n' || c == '\r') {
1147	      if (_nmea_len > 6) {
1148	        _nmea_buf[_nmea_len] = '\0';
1149	        if (parseNmeaSentence(_nmea_buf)) {
1150	          ret_status = e_worker_data_read;
1151	          if (!first_fix_logged && data.location_valid) {
1152	            first_fix_logged = true;
1153	            M5_LOGI("GNSS: NMEA fix — lat=%.5f lon=%.5f alt=%.1fm sats=%d",
1154	                    data.latitude, data.longitude, data.altitudeMSL, data.satsInView);
1155	          }
1156	        }
1157	      }
1158	      _nmea_len = 0;
1159	    } else if (_nmea_len > 0 && _nmea_len < (uint8_t)(sizeof(_nmea_buf) - 1)) {
1160	      _nmea_buf[_nmea_len++] = c;
1161	    }
1162	  }
1163	
1164	  if (location_timer.isExpired()) data.location_valid = false;
1165	  if (time_timer.isExpired()) {
1166	    data.time_valid = false;
1167	    data.hour = GPS_INVALID_HOUR;
1168	    data.minute = GPS_INVALID_MINUTE;
1169	    data.second = GPS_INVALID_SECOND;
1170	  }
1171	  if (date_timer.isExpired()) {
1172	    data.date_valid = false;
1173	    data.year  = GPS_INVALID_YEAR;
1174	    data.month = GPS_INVALID_MONTH;
1175	    data.day   = GPS_INVALID_DAY;
1176	  }
1177	
1178	  ApiDataCache::instance().update(data);
1179	  return ret_status;
1180	}
1181

64	bool GpsConnector::activate(bool retry) {
65	  // From Sparkfun examples/Example12_UseUart
66	  // Assume that the U-Blox GNSS is running at 9600 baud (the default) or at 38400 baud.
67	  if (!retry) {
68	#ifdef CONFIG_IDF_TARGET_ESP32S3 // Core S3SE fix using the correct serial pins
69	    _serial_conn.begin(38400, SERIAL_8N1, RXD2, TXD2);
70	#else
71	    _serial_conn.begin(38400);
72	#endif
73	
74	    _init_at = millis();
75	  }
76	  if (_tried_115200_at == 0 && (millis() - _init_at > 1000)) { // Wait for device to completely startup
77	    _tried_115200_at = millis();
78	    _serial_conn.updateBaudRate(115200);
79	    M5_LOGD("GNSS: Try at 115200 baud");
80	    if (_gnss.begin(_serial_conn, 500)) {
81	      M5_LOGD("GNSS: connected at 115200 baud"); // no need to set module to 38.4
82	    }
83	    else {
84	      return false;
85	    }
86	  }
87	  else if (_tried_38400_at == 0 && _tried_115200_at > 0 && (millis() - _tried_115200_at > 1200)) { // Wait for device to completely startup
88	    _tried_38400_at = millis();
89	    _serial_conn.updateBaudRate(38400);
90	    M5_LOGD("GNSS: Try at 38400 baud");
91	    if (_gnss.begin(_serial_conn, 500)) {
92	      M5_LOGD("GNSS: connected at 38400 baud"); // no need to set module to 38.4
93	    }
94	    else {
95	      return false;
96	    }
97	  }
98	  else if (_tried_9600_at == 0 && _tried_38400_at > 0 && (millis() - _tried_38400_at > 1200)) {
99	    _tried_9600_at = millis();
100	    _serial_conn.updateBaudRate(9600);
101	    M5_LOGD("GNSS: Try at 9600 baud");
102	    if (_gnss.begin(_serial_conn, 1000)) {
103	      M5_LOGD("GNSS: connected at 9600 baud, switching to 38400");
104	      _gnss.setSerialRate(38400);
105	      delay(100); // recovery time for gnss module baud rate change
106	      _serial_conn.updateBaudRate(38400);
107	    }
108	    else {
109	      return false;
110	    }
111	  }
112	  else if (_tried_4800_at == 0 && _tried_9600_at > 0 && (millis() - _tried_9600_at > 1200)) {
113	    _tried_4800_at = millis();
114	    _serial_conn.updateBaudRate(4800);
115	    M5_LOGD("GNSS: Try at 4800 baud");
116	    if (_gnss.begin(_serial_conn, 1000)) {
117	      M5_LOGD("GNSS: connected at 4800 baud, switching to 38400");
118	      _gnss.setSerialRate(38400);
119	      delay(100);
120	      _serial_conn.updateBaudRate(38400);
121	    }
122	    else {
123	      return false;
124	    }
125	  }
126	  else if (!_raw_dump_done && _tried_4800_at > 0 && (millis() - _tried_4800_at > 1200)) {
127	    // All UBX baud-rate attempts failed — test TX line and check for UBX response
128	    _raw_dump_done = true;
129	    _serial_conn.updateBaudRate(9600);
130	    while (_serial_conn.available()) _serial_conn.read(); // flush RX
131	    // Send a raw UBX-CFG-PRT poll (class 0x06, id 0x06, payload = UART1 port id 0x01)
132	    // B5 62 06 06 01 00 01 0E 24
133	    const uint8_t ubxPoll[] = {0xB5, 0x62, 0x06, 0x06, 0x01, 0x00, 0x01, 0x0E, 0x24};
134	    _serial_conn.write(ubxPoll, sizeof(ubxPoll));
135	    _serial_conn.flush();
136	    delay(300); // wait for GPS to respond
137	    uint8_t buf[128];
138	    int n = 0;
139	    uint32_t t = millis();
140	    while (n < (int)sizeof(buf) && (millis() - t) < 300) {
141	      if (_serial_conn.available()) buf[n++] = _serial_conn.read();
142	    }
143	    if (n > 0) {
144	      bool hasUbx = false;
145	      bool hasNmea = false;
146	      for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
147	        if (i < n - 1 && buf[i] == 0xB5 && buf[i+1] == 0x62) hasUbx = true;
148	        if (buf[i] == '$') hasNmea = true;
149	      }
150	      if (hasNmea && !hasUbx) {
151	        M5_LOGI("GNSS: GPS module: NMEA-only (TX not connected) — u-blox or compatible, 9600 baud");
152	        // Serial is already at 9600 baud on RXD2/TXD2 — just flush and switch mode.
153	        // Re-calling end()/begin() corrupts the UART on ESP32S3.
154	        delay(100);
155	        while (_serial_conn.available()) _serial_conn.read(); // flush stale bytes
156	        _nmea_mode = true;
157	        data.nmea_mode = true;
158	        data.nmea_sat_count = 0;
159	        memset(_nmea_buf, 0, sizeof(_nmea_buf));
160	        _nmea_len = 0;
161	        _nmea_used_count = 0;
162	        return true;
163	      }

124	const __FlashStringHelper* BaseScreen::get_error_message(const worker_map_t& workers, const handler_map_t& handlers) const {
125	  if (required_tube && millis() > 6000 && !workers.worker<GeigerCounter>(k_worker_gm_sensor)->active()) {
126	    return STATUS_ERROR_GEIGER;
127	  }
128	  if (required_gps && !workers.worker<GpsConnector>(k_worker_gps_connector)->active()) {
129	    return STATUS_ERROR_GPS;
130	  }
131	  if (required_sd && !SDInterface::i().can_write_logs()) {
132	    return STATUS_ERROR_SD;
133	  }
134	  if (required_wifi && WiFiWrapper_i.status() == WL_NO_SSID_AVAIL) {
135	    return STATUS_ERROR_WIFI_NO_SSID_AVAIL;
136	  }
137	  if (required_wifi && WiFiWrapper_i.status() == WL_CONNECT_FAILED) {
138	    return STATUS_ERROR_WIFI_CONNECT_FAILED;
139	  }
140	  if (required_wifi && WiFiWrapper_i.status() == WL_CONNECTION_LOST) {
141	    return STATUS_ERROR_WIFI_CONNECTION_LOST;
142	  }
143	
144	  return nullptr;

2	// Created by Jelle Bouwhuis on 12/27/19.

5	#ifndef SENSOR_HANDLER_INCLUDE_ACTIVATABLE_HPP_
6	#define SENSOR_HANDLER_INCLUDE_ACTIVATABLE_HPP_
7	
8	/**
9	 * Some abstract class used by the receiver and observer
10	 */
11	class Activatable {
12	 public:
13	  typedef enum State {
14	    e_state_inactive,
15	    e_state_active,
16	    e_state_activating_failed,
17	  } State;
18	
19	  Activatable();
20	
21	  /**
22	   * Returns true if worker/handler is active
23	   * @return
24	   */
25	  bool active() const;
26	
27	  /**
28	   * Returns current state of the worker/handler
29	   * @return
30	   */
31	  State get_active_state() const;
32	
33	  /**
34	   * Activates / deactivates the data worker/handler. If the activate / deactivate fails, status wont change
35	   * Requires the data handler to be initialized
36	   * @param active: true to activate, false to deactivate
37	   */
38	  virtual bool set_active(bool _activate) final;
39	
40	 protected:
41	
42	  /**
43	   * Initializes this (called once, when registered in the aggregator)
44	   */
45	  virtual void initialize() {}
46	
47	  /**
48	   * Perform an activate function, will be called when set_active(true)
49	   * Override this when the data handler will be activated/deactivated in times. The deactivate can clean up.
50	   * Example, use of wifi or bluetooth connection
51	   * @return true if activate was success. if false, object will not be activated (and wont be used)
52	   */
53	  virtual bool activate(bool retry);
54	
55	  /**
56	   * Perform an deactivate function, will be called when set_active(false)
57	   * Override this to cleanup the activation... shut down connections or other
58	   */
59	  virtual void deactivate();
60	
61	 private:
62	  State active_state;
63	};
64	
65	#endif //SENSOR_HANDLER_INCLUDE_ACTIVATABLE_HPP_
66

1	//
2	// Created by Jelle Bouwhuis on 12/27/19.
3	//
4	
5	#include <Activatable.hpp>
6	
7	Activatable::Activatable(): active_state(e_state_inactive) {
8	
9	}
10	
11	bool Activatable::set_active(bool _activate) {
12	  if(active_state == e_state_inactive && _activate) {
13	    active_state = activate(false) ? e_state_active : e_state_activating_failed;
14	    return active_state == e_state_active;
15	  } else if(active_state == e_state_activating_failed && _activate) {
16	    active_state = activate(true) ? e_state_active : e_state_activating_failed;
17	    return active_state == e_state_active;
18	  } else if(active_state != e_state_inactive && !_activate) {
19	    deactivate();
20	    active_state = e_state_inactive;
21	    return true;
22	  }
23	  return false;
24	}
25	
26	bool Activatable::activate(bool retry) {
27	  return true;
28	}
29	
30	void Activatable::deactivate() {}
31	
32	bool Activatable::active() const {
33	  return active_state == e_state_active;
34	}
35	
36	Activatable::State Activatable::get_active_state() const {
37	  return active_state;
38	}
39

1	/** @brief Manage position fix and date-time from GNSS receiver. 
2	 * 
3	 * Based on u-blox 8 receiver integrated Beitian BN-880 module, using the UBX communications
4	 * protocol instead of the usual NMEA-183. The problem with NMEA is that it was really 
5	 * designed for boats, where close enough to see with binoculars was good enough (well, not
6	 * really but it makes for a good story). The real issue is that it's hard to get consistent
7	 * fix and time data by parsing NMEA sentences.
8	 * 
9	*/
10

25	  // gps_connector doesn't have a new fix because it only becomes available
26	  // every 1 second, then the corresponding Boolean becomes false.
27	  // If stale, render in gray/white text. 
28	  bool location_valid;
29	  bool date_valid;
30	  bool time_valid;
31	
32	  bool valid() const {
33	    return location_valid && date_valid && time_valid;
34	  }

46	  };

21	BaseScreen* BootScreen::handle_input(Controller& controller, const worker_map_t& workers) {
22	  if (millis() > 6000 + _entered_at) {
23	    const auto& settings = workers.worker<LocalStorage>(k_worker_local_storage);
24	    if (controller.get_data().sd_card_status != SDInterface::SdStatus::e_sd_config_status_ok) {
25	      return &SdMessageScreen_i;
26	    } else if (!settings->get_device_id()) {
27	      return &FirstTimeStartupScreen_i;
28	    } else {
29	      return &DefaultEntryScreen_i;
30	    }
31	  }
32	  return nullptr;
33	}
34	
35	void BootScreen::enter_screen(Controller& controller) {
36	  _entered_at = millis();
37	}
38	
39	void BootScreen::leave_screen(Controller& controller) {
40	}
41	
42	void BootScreen::render(const worker_map_t& workers, const handler_map_t& handlers, bool force) {
43	  // Display something
44	
45	  const auto& storage = workers.worker<LocalStorage>(k_worker_local_storage);
46	  const auto& rtc = workers.worker<DateTimeProvider>(k_worker_rtc_connector);
47	
48	  // Display title
49	  M5.Lcd.setCursor(10, 10);
50	  M5.Lcd.setTextColor(LCD_COLOR_STALE_INCOMPLETE, LCD_COLOR_BACKGROUND);
51	  M5.Lcd.drawString("bGeigie Zen", 95, 50, &fonts::Font4);
52	  
53	  // Display version prominently
54	  M5.Lcd.setTextColor(LCD_COLOR_ACTIVITY, LCD_COLOR_BACKGROUND);
55	  M5.Lcd.drawString(BOOT_VERSION_STRING, 95, 85, &fonts::Font2);
56	  
57	  // Display user info
58	  M5.Lcd.setTextColor(LCD_COLOR_DEFAULT, LCD_COLOR_BACKGROUND);
59	  M5.Lcd.setCursor(5, 125);
60	  M5.Lcd.setFont(&fonts::Font2);
61	  M5.Lcd.printf("User name: %s", storage->get_user_name());
62	  M5.Lcd.setCursor(5, 143);
63	  M5.Lcd.printf("Device id: %d", storage->get_device_id());
64	  
65	  // Display date and time if available
66	  if (rtc && rtc->is_fresh()) {
67	    const auto& dt = rtc->get_data();
68	    M5.Lcd.setCursor(5, 163);
69	    M5.Lcd.printf("Date: %04d-%02d-%02d", dt.year, dt.month, dt.day);
70	    M5.Lcd.setCursor(5, 183);
71	    M5.Lcd.printf("Time: %02d:%02d:%02d", dt.hour, dt.minute, dt.second);
72	  }
73	
74	  // Display safecast copyright
75	  M5.Lcd.setTextFont(1);
76	  M5.Lcd.setTextColor(LCD_COLOR_DEFAULT, LCD_COLOR_BACKGROUND);
77	  M5.Lcd.drawString("SAFECAST", 230, 215, &fonts::Font0);
78	  M5.Lcd.setTextColor(LCD_COLOR_STALE_INCOMPLETE, LCD_COLOR_BACKGROUND);
79	  M5.Lcd.drawString(COPYRIGHT_YEAR_STRING, 285, 215, &fonts::Font0);
80	}

> AGENT

I've traced through the full boot sequence and rendering logic. Here's what's actually happening:

1. Boot screen (6 seconds, no status bar) → GPS activates in NMEA mode at ~5.8s
2. Main screen appears → status bar renders immediately showing GPS0 (orange)
3. During cold start (30-60s), the u-blox M7 sends `$GPGSV,1,1,00` — literally 0 satellites visible
4. GPS goes 0 visible → fix almost simultaneously (no intermediate count visible)
5. Mode switch "instant fix" = GPS acquired its fix during survey mode visit; the screen `clear()` + re-render shows it for the first time

The real UX problem: **GPS0 (orange) is ambiguous** — is the GPS working but searching, or broken? When numSV=0, showing "GPS?" would communicate "searching" much more clearly. Here's the fix:

> AGENT

These are the same IDE false positives we've seen before — the clang analyzer can't resolve `get_data()` on the template-based worker, but GCC compiles it fine. The existing `gps->get_data().location_valid` on the adjacent line has the same "issue" and already compiled successfully.

The behavior change is now:

| State | Display | Color |
|-------|---------|-------|
| GPS inactive | `GPS` | red/inactive |
| Active, 0 satellites visible (cold start) | `GPS?` | orange |
| Active, satellites visible, no fix | `GPS12` | orange |
| Fix acquired | `GPS5` | green |

This makes cold-start searching clearly distinct from "GPS is failing" (red) and from "GPS is tracking but not fixed yet" (orange with count). The `?` communicates "working but searching" rather than the confusing `0` that looked like a count.

The status bar does auto-update every 1000ms — what you were seeing (GPS0 → instant green after survey mode) was the GPS cold start naturally completing during the time spent in survey mode. The mode switch just happened to trigger the re-render that showed the new state first. With no satellites visible for 30-60 seconds at cold start, you'll now see `GPS?` (orange) → `GPS12` (orange) → `GPS5` (green) as the GPS acquires lock.

978	bool GpsConnector::parseNmeaSentence(const char* sentence) {
979	  // Validate checksum (XOR of bytes between '$' and '*')
980	  const char* star = strchr(sentence, '*');
981	  if (star != nullptr && strlen(star) >= 3) {
982	    uint8_t calc = 0;
983	    for (const char* p = sentence + 1; p < star; p++) calc ^= (uint8_t)*p;
984	    uint8_t recv = (uint8_t)strtol(star + 1, nullptr, 16);
985	    if (calc != recv) return false;
986	  }
987	
988	  bool isRmc = (strncmp(sentence, "$GPRMC", 6) == 0 || strncmp(sentence, "$GNRMC", 6) == 0);
989	  bool isGga = (strncmp(sentence, "$GPGGA", 6) == 0 || strncmp(sentence, "$GNGGA", 6) == 0);
990	  bool isGsv = (strncmp(sentence, "$GPGSV", 6) == 0 || strncmp(sentence, "$GLGSV", 6) == 0 || strncmp(sentence, "$GNGSV", 6) == 0);
991	  bool isGsa = (strncmp(sentence, "$GPGSA", 6) == 0 || strncmp(sentence, "$GNGSA", 6) == 0);
992	  if (!isRmc && !isGga && !isGsv && !isGsa) return false;
993	
994	  // Determine constellation type char from sentence prefix for GSV
995	  char gnssIdType = 'G'; // default GPS
996	  if (strncmp(sentence, "$GL", 3) == 0) gnssIdType = 'R'; // GLONASS
997	  else if (strncmp(sentence, "$GA", 3) == 0) gnssIdType = 'E'; // Galileo
998	  else if (strncmp(sentence, "$GB", 3) == 0) gnssIdType = 'B'; // BeiDou
999	
1000	  // Copy and strip checksum for field splitting
1001	  char buf[128];
1002	  strncpy(buf, sentence, sizeof(buf) - 1);
1003	  buf[sizeof(buf) - 1] = '\0';
1004	  char* s = strchr(buf, '*');
1005	  if (s) *s = '\0';
1006	
1007	  // Split on commas (GPGSV needs up to 20 fields, GPGSA up to 18)
1008	  char* fields[20] = {nullptr};
1009	  int nf = 0;
1010	  char* p = buf;
1011	  while (nf < 20) {
1012	    fields[nf++] = p;
1013	    p = strchr(p, ',');
1014	    if (!p) break;
1015	    *p++ = '\0';
1016	  }

11	  class Timer {
12	    public:
13	      Timer();                              // constructor with zero timeout, starts in "expired" state by default
14	      Timer(unsigned long value);           // overloaded constructor: provide a setTimeout in milliseconds, starts in "expired" state by default
15	      void setTimeout(unsigned long value); // set/change how long the timer will run until it expires in milliseconds
16	      void setHertz(int value);             // set/change how many times onRestart will return true in one second
17	      void restart();                       // reset and start the timer
18	      void stop();                          // stop the timer
19	      bool isActive();                      // check if time is left
20	      bool isExpired();                     // returns true if time has run out
21	      bool isStopped();                     // returns true if the timer is stopped
22	      bool onRestart();                     // returns true if the timer is expired and restarts the timer automatically
23	      bool onActive();                      // returns true once the timer is active, then waits for it to expire and go active again
24	      bool onExpired();                     // returns true once the timer is expired, then waits for it to go active and expire again
25	      unsigned long getTimeout();           // returns the value of setTimeout, which is how long the timer will run until it expires in milliseconds
26	      int getHertz();                       // returns the value of setHertz, which is how many times onRestart will return true in one second
27	      unsigned long getValue();             // how many milliseconds that have passed since the start of the timer
28	      unsigned long getInverseValue();      // how many milliseconds the timer has until finished
29	      int getPercentValue();                // how much time has passed as a percentage of the interval
30	      int getInversePercentValue();         // how much time is left as a percentage of the interval
31	    private:
32	      unsigned long _timeout  = 0;          // how long this timer should run for
33	      unsigned long _waypoint = 0;          // the point in time the timer was started or reset
34	      int _hertz              = 0;          // the value given to setHertz
35	      bool _has_been_active   = false;      // helps fire the onActive event only once
36	      bool _has_been_expired  = false;      // helps fire the onExpired event only once
37	      void _updateState();                  // maintains timer current state, helps eliminate rollover issues
38	      enum {ACTIVE, EXPIRED, STOPPED} _state = EXPIRED;  // timer internal states, constructed in "expired" state by default
39	  };
40	}

613	bool GpsConnector::setDynamicModel(UbxDynamicModel model, bool saveToFlash) {
614	  bool success = false;
615	  M5_LOGD("GNSS: Setting dynamic model to %d (saveToFlash: %d)", model, saveToFlash);
616	
617	  if (data.protocolVersionHigh > 0 && data.protocolVersionHigh < 18) {
618	    // u-blox 7 (e.g. UBX-G7020-KT): use UBX-CFG-NAV5 (0x06/0x24)
619	    // CFG-VALSET is not available on protocol versions below 18
620	    uint8_t nav5Payload[36] = {0};
621	    nav5Payload[0] = 0x01; // mask LSB: apply dynModel only
622	    nav5Payload[1] = 0x00; // mask MSB
623	    nav5Payload[2] = (uint8_t)model; // dynModel
624	    nav5Payload[3] = 0x03; // fixMode: auto 2D/3D
625	    if (sendUBXMessage(UBX_CLASS_CFG, UBX_ID_CFG_NAV5, nav5Payload, sizeof(nav5Payload))) {
626	      delay(100);
627	      _current_model = model;
628	      success = true;
629	    }
630	  } else {
631	    // u-blox 8+ (M8/M9/M10): use UBX-CFG-VALSET (0x06/0x8A)
632	    uint8_t msgPayload[12] = {0};
633	    msgPayload[0] = 0x00; // version 0
634	    msgPayload[1] = saveToFlash ? UBX_CFG_LAYER_ALL : UBX_CFG_LAYER_RAM;
635	    // Key: CFG-NAVSPG-DYNMODEL (0x20110021)
636	    msgPayload[4] = 0x21;
637	    msgPayload[5] = 0x00;
638	    msgPayload[6] = 0x11;
639	    msgPayload[7] = 0x20;
640	    msgPayload[8] = (uint8_t)model;
641	    if (sendUBXMessage(UBX_CLASS_CFG, UBX_CFG_VALSET, msgPayload, sizeof(msgPayload))) {
642	      delay(100);
643	      _current_model = model;
644	      success = true;
645	    }
646	  }
647	
648	  if (success) {
649	    const char* modelName = "UNKNOWN";
650	    switch(model) {
651	      case DYNMODEL_PORT: modelName = "PORTABLE"; break;
652	      case DYNMODEL_STATIONARY: modelName = "STATIONARY"; break;
653	      case DYNMODEL_PEDESTRIAN: modelName = "PEDESTRIAN"; break;
654	      case DYNMODEL_AUTOMOTIVE: modelName = "AUTOMOTIVE"; break;
655	      case DYNMODEL_SEA: modelName = "SEA"; break;
656	      case DYNMODEL_AIRBORNE_1G: modelName = "AIRBORNE 1G"; break;
657	      case DYNMODEL_AIRBORNE_2G: modelName = "AIRBORNE 2G"; break;
658	      case DYNMODEL_AIRBORNE_4G: modelName = "AIRBORNE 4G"; break;
659	      case DYNMODEL_WRIST: modelName = "WRIST"; break;
660	    }
661	    M5_LOGD("GNSS: Set to %s mode (saveToFlash: %d)", modelName, saveToFlash);
662	  } else {
663	    M5_LOGD("GNSS: Failed to send dynamic model change command");
664	  }
665	
666	  return success;
667	}
668

842	bool GpsConnector::sendUBXMessage(uint8_t msgClass, uint8_t msgID, const uint8_t* payload, size_t payloadSize) {
843	  // UBX message structure:
844	  // Sync Char 1: 0xB5
845	  // Sync Char 2: 0x62
846	  // Class: 1 byte
847	  // ID: 1 byte
848	  // Length: 2 bytes (little endian)
849	  // Payload: variable length
850	  // Checksum: 2 bytes (CK_A, CK_B)
851	  
852	  // Write sync chars
853	  _serial_conn.write(UBX_SYNC_CHAR_1);
854	  _serial_conn.write(UBX_SYNC_CHAR_2);
855	  
856	  // Write class and ID
857	  _serial_conn.write(msgClass);
858	  _serial_conn.write(msgID);
859	  
860	  // Write length (little endian)
861	  _serial_conn.write(payloadSize & 0xFF);
862	  _serial_conn.write((payloadSize >> 8) & 0xFF);
863	  
864	  // Write payload
865	  if (payload != nullptr && payloadSize > 0) {
866	    _serial_conn.write(payload, payloadSize);
867	  }
868	
869	  // Calculate checksum
870	  uint8_t ck_a = 0, ck_b = 0;
871	
872	  // Add class, ID, and length to checksum
873	  ck_a += msgClass;
874	  ck_b += ck_a;
875	
876	  ck_a += msgID;
877	  ck_b += ck_a;
878	
879	  ck_a += payloadSize & 0xFF;
880	  ck_b += ck_a;
881

923	/**
924	 * Ask the GNSS to enter Backup mode (save state) using UBX-RXM-PMREQ.
925	 * This dramatically reduces TTFF on the next boot.
926	 */
927	bool GpsConnector::requestBackup() {
928	  // UBX-RXM-PMREQ (0x02 0x41) payload is 8 bytes:
929	  // 0-1: version / reserved (set to 0)
930	  // 2-3: duration (little-endian) – 0 means indefinite
931	  // 4-7: flags – bit0 = backup, everything else 0
932	  const uint8_t payload[8] = { 0x00, 0x00, // version / reserved
933	                               0x00, 0x00, // duration LSB/MSB (0 = indefinitely)
934	                               0x01, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00 }; // flags (backup)
935	  return sendUBXMessage(0x02 /*RXM*/, 0x41 /*PMREQ*/, payload, sizeof(payload));
936	}

> AGENT

The bug is that `gnss.checkUblox()` in `loop()` drains the Serial2 UART buffer before `produceDataNmea()` can read it. Let me verify and check for a guard condition:

> TOOL

> AGENT

**Found the root cause.** `checkUbloxInternal()` has a `while(serialPort->available())` tight loop — it drains the entire Serial2 UART buffer on every loop iteration. In NMEA mode, that consumes all incoming NMEA sentences before `produceDataNmea()` ever sees them. The fix is one line in `main.cpp`:

> TOOL

> AGENT

That's the fix. The full chain:

1. `checkUbloxInternal()` loops `while(serialPort->available())` — every byte consumed
2. GPS sends NMEA at 9600 baud → all bytes eaten by `checkUblox()` before `produceDataNmea()` runs
3. `produceDataNmea()` finds nothing → `location_valid` stays false forever → `GPS?`

With the fix, `checkUblox()` is skipped in NMEA mode, leaving Serial2 data for `produceDataNmea()` to parse. For M8/M9/M10 (UBX mode), behaviour is unchanged.

The mode-switch "fix" was likely coincidental timing: survey mode's longer render loop (SD writes) occasionally let a complete NMEA sentence slip through in the small window between `checkUblox()` and `produce_data()`, giving a one-time valid reading.

> AGENT

Go ahead and flash it. If the fix is correct you should see `GPS?` transition to `GPS5` (green) within a few seconds of boot — no mode switching needed.

> DEVELOPER

Yes, that works now nicely!!

> AGENT

Excellent! That was a subtle but critical bug — `gnss.checkUblox()` was silently starving the NMEA parser on every single loop iteration.

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/rob/.claude/projects/-home-rob-Documents-Safecast-bGeigieZen/memory/feedback_nmea_checkublox.md",
  "content": "---\nname: GPS NMEA mode / checkUblox starvation bug\ndescription: gnss.checkUblox() drains Serial2 before produceDataNmea() can read NMEA sentences — must guard with !nmea_mode\ntype: feedback\n---\n\nIn `loop()`, `gnss.checkUblox()` calls `checkUbloxInternal()` which does `while(serialPort->available()) processIncomingByte(serialPort->read())` — draining the ENTIRE Serial2 buffer on every loop iteration. In NMEA fallback mode (u-blox M7, TX-only wiring), the GPS sends NMEA sentences that get consumed by checkUblox before `produceDataNmea()` sees them, so `location_valid` stays false forever.\n\n**Fix applied in `bgeigiezen_firmware/main.cpp`:**\n```cpp\nif (gps.active() && !gps.get_data().nmea_mode) {\n    gnss.checkUblox();\n}\n```\n\n**Why:** checkUblox() is needed for UBX mode (M8/M9/M10) to receive async NAV-PVT packets. But in NMEA mode, it starves the NMEA parser.\n\n**How to apply:** Any time checkUblox() is called in the main loop, it must be guarded by `!nmea_mode`. The symptom was GPS showing `GPS?` (orange, 0 sats) indefinitely at boot, only \"fixing\" accidentally when mode-switch timing let one sentence slip through.\n"
}
```

1	# bGeigieZen
2	
3	A modern radiation monitoring device based on the M5Stack hardware platform, developed by the Safecast community.
4	
5	## Overview
6	
7	bGeigieZen is a portable radiation monitoring device that combines precision sensing with a user-friendly interface. Built on the M5Stack platform, it provides real-time radiation measurements with GPS logging capabilities.
8	![from website](https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/a8260427-227d-4a53-8914-8a7f9e7631ee)
9	
10	## Features
11	
12	- Real-time radiation monitoring
13	- GPS location tracking
14	- Data logging to SD card
15	- User-friendly display interface
16	- Long battery life
17	- Mobile app connectivity
18	- Compatible with the Safecast API
19	
20	## Hardware Requirements
21	
22	- M5Stack Core device (Core2 or CoreS3)
23	- LND-7317 radiation sensor
24	- GPS module (see [Supported GPS Modules](#supported-gps-modules) below)
25	- SD card for logging
26	
27	## Supported GPS Modules
28	
29	The firmware supports u-blox GPS modules over UART (Port C on M5Stack).
30	
31	| Module | Chip | Protocol | Mode | Notes |
32	|---|---|---|---|---|
33	| Beitian BN-280 / BN-880 | u-blox M8 | UBX (bidirectional) | Full UBX | Default recommended module |
34	| u-blox NEO-M9N | u-blox M9 | UBX (bidirectional) | Full UBX | |
35	| u-blox NEO-M10 | u-blox M10 | UBX (bidirectional) | Full UBX | |
36	| u-blox NEO-7M / UBX-G7020-KT | u-blox M7 | NMEA (RX-only) or UBX | NMEA fallback or Full UBX | RX-only works with only GPS TX → CoreS3 GPIO18 connected; connect GPS RX → GPIO17 for full UBX |
37	
38	### Wiring (M5Stack CoreS3 Port C)
39	
40	| CoreS3 Port C pin | GPIO | Direction | Connect to |
41	|---|---|---|---|
42	| RXD | GPIO18 | ESP32 receives | GPS TX |
43	| TXD | GPIO17 | ESP32 transmits | GPS RX (optional — required for full UBX mode) |
44	
45	**NMEA fallback mode:** If only the GPS TX → GPIO18 wire is connected (GPS RX is not wired), the firmware automatically detects this and switches to NMEA-only parsing. Coordinates, time, date, and satellite count are still reported. Full UBX mode (both wires) is recommended for faster cold-start and richer data.
46	
47	## Software Setup
48	
49	1. **Install Required Development environment**
50	   - VS Code with PlatformIO or PlatformIO standalone
51	
52	2. **Install Required Libraries**:
53	   - m5stack/M5Unified
54	   - claypuppet/SensorReporter
55	   - beakes/TeenyUbloxConnect
56	   - alextaujenis/RBD_Timer
57	
58	3. **Clone the Repository**:
59	   ```bash
60	   git clone https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieZen.git
61	   ```
62	
63	## Building and Flashing
64	
65	1. Open the project in VS Code with PlatformIO, or invoke PlatformIO from the shell/CMD.
66	2. Select your M5Stack board.
67	3. Compile and upload.
68	
69	## Usage
70	
71	1. Power on the device.
72	2. Wait for GPS signal acquisition.
73	3. Radiation measurements will display on screen.
74	4. Data logs automatically save to the SD card.
75	
76	## SD card configuration files
77	
78	This project references configuration files used by both bGeigie Nano and bGeigie Zen devices. They are different and not interchangeable. Use the appropriate file for your device:
79	
80	### bGeigie Nano — `SAFECAST.TXT`
81	
82	- Location: SD card root of a bGeigie Nano.
83	- Format: `key=value` per line. Example reference file: https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieNanoKit/blob/master/SD%20card/SAFECAST.TXT
84	
85	Common keys (with firmware semantics):
86	- `nm` — Profile name. Typically `SAFECAST`. Informational label, shown in menus/log headers.
87	- `did` — Device ID used by the Safecast ecosystem to associate logs/uploads. Required for proper identification. Range and format depend on Nano firmware and Safecast policy.
88	- `gt` — GPS type selector. `0` often means u‑blox; other values map to alternative GPS chipsets/modules. Used to configure serial baud, message set, and initialization.
89	- `gm` — GPS mode. Controls GPS operating/profile mode (e.g., cold/warm start preferences or power mode). Exact mapping is firmware‑specific.
90	- `cpmf` — CPM calibration factor (numeric). Multiplier applied to raw tube counts to compute calibrated CPM or uSv/h conversion path.
91	- `cpmn` — CPM factor name (text). Label for the calibration factor (e.g., `Cs137`) stored alongside logs.
92	- `bqmf` — Bq/m² calibration factor (numeric). Multiplier used when exporting in Bq/m² units.
93	- `bqmn` — Bq/m² factor name (text). Label for the Bq/m² factor (e.g., `Cs137`).
94	- `al` — Alert threshold in CPM. When current CPM ≥ `al`, the Nano emits an alert (beep/visual), depending on sound settings.
95	- `tz` — Timezone offset in hours from UTC. Example: `9` for JST. Used for local time stamping on device.
96	- `cn` — Country code (ISO‑3166). Often `JPN`, etc. May be embedded in file headers or used for region‑specific defaults.
97	- `st` — Logging start mode. `0` typically manual; non‑zero values may enable auto‑start or different trigger logic. Firmware‑dependent.
98	- `ss` — Sound setting. `0` off, `1` on (beeps/clicks). May offer additional levels on some firmware.
99	- `sh` — Screen brightness (0–100). Backlight level.
100	- `sm` — Screen/map mode. Selects UI layout or mapping behavior. Firmware‑dependent.
101	- `cw` — Commit/write interval in seconds. Controls how frequently log lines are flushed to SD.
102	
103	Notes:
104	- Values and enumerations above reflect common Nano firmwares; refer to the Nano documentation for the definitive mapping.
105	- Consult the Nano repository for full semantics and latest defaults.
106	
107	Sample templates:
108	
109	- Minimal `SAFECAST.TXT` (Nano):
110	
111	```ini
112	nm=SAFECAST
113	did=5555
114	al=100
115	tz=9
116	ss=1
117	cw=60
118	```
119	
120	- Full `SAFECAST.TXT` example (Nano):
121	
122	```ini
123	nm=SAFECAST
124	did=5555
125	gt=0
126	gm=0
127	cpmf=334
128	cpmn=Cs137
129	bqmf=37
130	bqmn=Cs137
131	al=100
132	tz=9
133	cn=JPN
134	st=0
135	ss=1
136	sh=100
137	sm=0
138	cw=60
139	```
140	
141	References:
142	- Canonical example file in Nano kit: https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieNanoKit/blob/master/SD%20card/SAFECAST.TXT
143	- Nano project docs and firmware should be consulted for exact enumerations and behavior.
144	
145	### bGeigie Zen — `SAFEZEN.txt`
146	
147	Every Zen device uses an SD card with a `SAFEZEN.txt` configuration file placed at the SD root. At minimum, you must set a valid `device_id` for the device to operate.
148	
149	Quick start:
150	- See sample files in `SDcard/`:
151	  - `SDcard/minimal/SAFEZEN.txt` (minimal config with just `device_id`)
152	  - `SDcard/all_settings/SAFEZEN.txt` (example of all commonly supported fields)
153	- You can also manage all settings from the device: Menu → Settings (the device can read and write `SAFEZEN.txt`).
154	
155	Supported settings (key=value per line):
156	- `version`
157	  - Optional firmware version marker written by the device. If missing, the current firmware attempts to parse using the latest format.
158	- `device_id`
159	  - Required. Must be between 5000 and 5999. The device will not operate without a valid ID in this range.
160	- `user_name`
161	  - Optional display and identification name used on-screen and in some outputs.
162	- `api_key`
163	  - Optional Safecast API key for uploading where applicable.
164	- `access_point_password`
165	  - Password for the device’s built-in Wi‑Fi Access Point (used by the configuration portal).
166	- `wifi_ssid`
167	  - Primary Wi‑Fi SSID for connecting to a local network.
168	- `wifi_password`
169	  - Password for `wifi_ssid`.
170	- `wifi_ssid2`
171	  - Secondary Wi‑Fi SSID profile (optional).
172	- `wifi_password2`
173	  - Password for `wifi_ssid2`.
174	- `wifi_profile`
175	  - Select active Wi‑Fi profile. `1` = use `wifi_ssid`/`wifi_password`. `2` = use `wifi_ssid2`/`wifi_password2`.
176	- `alert_threshold`
177	  - CPM alert threshold. Range 10–9999. Triggers audible and visual “CPM ALERT” when exceeded.
178	  - Note: Older examples may show `alarm_threshold`, but the current firmware uses `alert_threshold`.
179	  - See: [Zen Specification — Alerts](https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieZen/wiki/Specification)
180	- `display_cpm`
181	  - Display unit selector. `1` = show CPM. `0` = show µSv/h.
182	- `manual_logging`
183	  - Logging behavior. `1` = manual start/stop. `0` = automatic depending on mode/movement.
184	  - See: [Zen Specification — Logging](https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieZen/wiki/Specification)
185	- `enable_journal`
186	  - Enable the journal log. `1` = enabled, `0` = disabled.
187	  - See: [Zen Specification — Files & Journaling](https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieZen/wiki/Specification)
188	- `log_void`
189	  - Include invalid/void lines in logs. `1` = include, `0` = exclude.
190	- `screen_dim_timeout`
191	  - Seconds of inactivity before screen dims.
192	  - See: [Zen Specification — Display & Power](https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieZen/wiki/Specification)
193	- `screen_off_timeout`
194	  - Seconds of inactivity before screen turns off.
195	  - See: [Zen Specification — Display & Power](https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieZen/wiki/Specification)
196	- `animated_screensaver`
197	  - Screensaver animation on dim/off. `1` = enabled, `0` = disabled.
198	  - See: [Zen Specification — Display & Power](https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieZen/wiki/Specification)
199	- `error_alert_sound`
200	  - Error beep sounds. `1` = enabled, `0` = disabled.
201	  - See: [Zen Specification — Alerts](https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieZen/wiki/Specification)
202	- `dim_brightness`
203	  - Brightness level (0–100) used while dimmed/screensaver.
204	- `audio_volume`
205	  - Global audio volume (0–100) for clicks and alerts.
206	- `fixed_latitude`
207	  - Latitude used in Fixed mode.
208	- `fixed_longitude`
209	  - Longitude used in Fixed mode.
210	- `fixed_range`
211	  - Radius in km for Fixed mode validity.
212	- `dop_max`
213	  - Maximum acceptable Dilution of Precision (DOP) for GPS fix validity.
214	  - See: [Zen Specification — GPS & Accuracy](https://github.com/Safecast/bGeigieZen/wiki/Specification)
215	
216	Notes:
217	- Unknown or unsupported lines are ignored by the firmware.
218	- Settings changed from the device UI are persisted to internal memory and can be written back to `SAFEZEN.txt` via Settings → “Save to SD”.
219	- The device also supports configuring via Wi‑Fi using the built-in Access Point or a local network (see Settings screen).
220	
221	### Nano ↔ Zen quick comparison (overlapping concepts)
222	
223	| Concept | bGeigie Nano (SAFECAST.TXT) | bGeigie Zen (SAFEZEN.txt) |
224	| --- | --- | --- |
225	| Device ID | `did` (format/range Nano‑specific) | `device_id` (required, 5000–5999) |
226	| Alert threshold (CPM) | `al` | `alert_threshold` |
227	| Timezone | `tz` (hours offset) | not required; Zen uses RTC and can show local time; timezone not needed for logging |
228	| Display units | n/a (Nano UI dependent) | `display_cpm` (1=CPM, 0=µSv/h) |
229	| Wi‑Fi/API | n/a in Nano SAFECAST | `wifi_*`, `api_key`, `access_point_password` |
230	| Logging behavior | `st`/`cw` (start mode and commit interval) | `manual_logging` (manual vs auto), commit policy handled by firmware |
231	| GPS accuracy threshold | n/a (implicit) | `dop_max` (DOP limit) |
232	| Fixed location | n/a | `fixed_latitude`, `fixed_longitude`, `fixed_range` |
233	| Brightness & power | `sh` (0–100) | `dim_brightness`, `screen_dim_timeout`, `screen_off_timeout`, `animated_screensaver` |
234	| Sound & alerts | `ss` (0/1) | `error_alert_sound`, `audio_volume`, `alert_threshold` |
235	
236	Tip: These files are not interchangeable. Use `SAFECAST.TXT` on Nano, and `SAFEZEN.txt` on Zen.
237

Full UBX mode (both wires) is recommended for faster cold-start and richer data."
